is sufficiently large and
c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}
is sufficiently small.
The conclusion (
49
) follows from summing up the above inequalities.

For the remaining part of the lemma,
note that the following inequalities that bound the individual terms of (
49
) can be inferred from (
22
): namely,

\|\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma_{\star}\|_{\fro}\leq\sqrt{2r_{\star}}\|\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma_{\star}\|\leq\sqrt{2r_{\star}}(C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}^{2}\kappa^{2}+1)\|X_{\star}\|^{2}

by (
22d
), and

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{S}_{t}\|\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\|_{\fro}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{r_{\star}}\|\widetilde{S}_{t}\|\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{r_{\star}}(C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}\kappa\|X_{\star}\|)\cdot\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|\cdot\|\widetilde{S}_{t}\|\cdot\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{r_{\star}}(C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}\kappa\|X_{\star}\|)\cdot(c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}\kappa^{-C_{\delta}/2}\|X_{\star}\|)\cdot(C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}\kappa\|X_{\star}\|)\cdot\sigma_{\min}^{-1}(\Sigma_{\star})

\displaystyle=\sqrt{r_{\star}}c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}^{2}\kappa^{3}\|X_{\star}\|^{2}\kappa^{-C_{\delta}/2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{r_{\star}}C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}^{2}\|X_{\star}\|^{2},

where the first inequality uses the fact that

\widetilde{N}_{t}=N_{t}V_{t}
contains a rank-
r_{\star}
factor
V_{t}
,
hence has rank at most
r_{\star}
;
the second line follows from (
22d
), the third line follows from (
22c
) and (
22d
),
and the last line follows from choosing
c_{\delta}
sufficiently small
such that
c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}\leq 1
(which is possible since
c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}\lesssim c_{\delta}/c_{\lambda}^{3})

and from choosing
C_{\delta}\geq 6
such that
\kappa^{3}\kappa^{-C_{\delta}/2}\leq 1
.
Finally, from (
22b
) and its corollary (
24
), we have

2n\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|^{2}\leq 2n\alpha^{3/2}\|X_{\star}\|^{1/2}\leq\|X_{\star}\|^{2},

since from (
12c
) it is easy to show that

\alpha\leq(2n)^{-2/3}\|X_{\star}\|
.

Combining these inequalities and (
49
) yields

\|\Delta_{t}\|\leq 8\delta\sqrt{r_{\star}}(\sqrt{2}C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}^{2}\kappa^{2}+1+C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}^{2}+1)\|X_{\star}\|^{2}\leq 16\delta\sqrt{r_{\star}}\kappa^{2}(C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}^{2}+1)\|X_{\star}\|^{2}.

(52)

Recalling that by (
10
) we have

\delta\sqrt{r_{\star}}\kappa^{2}\leq c_{\delta}\kappa^{-C_{\delta}+2}\leq c_{\delta}\kappa^{-2C_{\delta}/3}

as long as
C_{\delta}\geq 6
, we obtain the desired conclusion.
We may choose
c_{\ref{lem:Delta-bound}}=32(C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}+1)^{2}c_{\delta}
, and the bound
c_{\ref{lem:Delta-bound}}\lesssim c_{\delta}/c_{\lambda}\lesssim c_{\delta}/c_{\lambda}^{3}

follows from
C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}\lesssim c_{\lambda}^{-1/2}
.
∎

We next present several useful decompositions of the signal term
S_{t+1}

and the noise term
N_{t+1}
, which are extremely useful in later developments.

Lemma 13
.

For any
t
such that
\widetilde{S}_{t}
is invertible and (
22
) holds,
we have

\displaystyle S_{t+1}

\displaystyle=\left((1-\eta)I+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I+{E}^{a}_{t})(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\right)\widetilde{S}_{t}V_{t}^{\top}+\eta{E}^{b}_{t},

(53a)

\displaystyle N_{t+1}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\left((1-\eta)\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I+\eta{E}^{c}_{t}\right)(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}V_{t}^{\top}

\displaystyle\qquad+\eta{E}^{e}_{t}(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}V_{t}^{\top}+\widetilde{O}_{t}V_{{t,\perp}}^{\top}+\eta{E}^{d}_{t},

(53b)

where the error terms satisfy

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{E}^{a}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\leq 2c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}\kappa^{-4}\|X_{\star}\|\cdot{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+2{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|},

(54a)

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{E}^{b}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|}{\sigma_{\min}(\widetilde{S}_{t})}\right)^{3/4}\sigma_{\min}(\widetilde{S}_{t})\leq\frac{1}{20}\kappa^{-10}\sigma_{\min}(\widetilde{S}_{t}),

(54b)

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{E}^{c}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa^{-4}\|X_{\star}\|\cdot{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|},

(54c)

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{E}^{d}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|}{\sigma_{\min}(\widetilde{S}_{t})}\right)^{3/4}\sigma_{\min}(\widetilde{S}_{t}),

(54d)

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{E}^{e}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\leq 2{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+c_{\ref{lem:Delta-bound}}\kappa^{-5}\|X_{\star}\|\cdot{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}.

(54e)

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\|{E}^{b}_{t}\|\leq\frac{1}{24C_{\max}\kappa}\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|,

(54f)

\displaystyle\|{E}^{d}_{t}\|\leq\frac{1}{24C_{\max}\kappa}\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|.

(54g)

Here,
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}
can either be the Frobenius norm or the spectral norm.

To proceed, we would need the approximate update equation of the rotated signal term
\widetilde{S}_{t+1}
, and the rotated misalignment term
\widetilde{N}_{t+1}\widetilde{S}_{t+1}^{-1}
later in the proof.
Since directly analyzing the evolution of these two terms seems challenging, we resort to two surrogate matrices
S_{t+1}V_{t}+S_{t+1}V_{{t,\perp}}Q
,
and
(N_{t+1}V_{t}+N_{t+1}V_{{t,\perp}}Q)(S_{t+1}V_{t}+S_{t+1}V_{{t,\perp}}Q)^{-1}
,
as documented in the following two lemmas.

Lemma 14
.

For any
t
such that
\widetilde{S}_{t}
is invertible and (
22
) holds, and any matrix
Q\in\mathbb{R}^{(r-r_{\star})\times r_{\star}}
with
\|Q\|\leq 2
,
we have

S_{t+1}V_{t}+S_{t+1}V_{{t,\perp}}Q=(I+\eta{E}^{\ref{lem:S-surrogate}}_{t})\left((1-\eta)I+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\right)\widetilde{S}_{t},

(55)

where
{E}^{\ref{lem:S-surrogate}}_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{\star}\times r_{\star}}
is a matrix (depending on
Q
) satisfying

\|{E}^{\ref{lem:S-surrogate}}_{t}\|\leq\frac{1}{200(C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}+1)^{4}\kappa^{5}}.

Here,
C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}>0
is given in Lemma
3
.

Lemma 15
.

For any
t
such that
\widetilde{S}_{t}
is invertible and (
22
) holds, and any matrix
Q\in\mathbb{R}^{(r-r_{\star})\times r_{\star}}
with
\|Q\|\leq 2
,
we have

\displaystyle(N_{t+1}V_{t}+N_{t+1}V_{{t,\perp}}Q)(S_{t+1}V_{t}+S_{t+1}V_{{t,\perp}}Q)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}(1+\eta{E}^{\ref{lem:NS-surrogate}.a}_{t})\big((1-\eta)\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I\big)\big((1-\eta)\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I+\eta\Sigma_{\star}^{2}\big)^{-1}(1+\eta{E}^{\ref{lem:S-surrogate}}_{t})^{-1}+\eta{E}^{\ref{lem:NS-surrogate}.b}_{t}

where
{E}^{\ref{lem:NS-surrogate}.a}_{t}
,
{E}^{\ref{lem:NS-surrogate}.b}_{t}
are matrices (depending on
Q
) satisfying

\displaystyle\|{E}^{\ref{lem:NS-surrogate}.a}_{t}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{200(C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}+1)^{4}\kappa^{5}},

(56a)

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{E}^{\ref{lem:NS-surrogate}.b}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\leq 400c_{\lambda}^{-1}\kappa^{2}\|X_{\star}\|^{-2}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\frac{1}{64(C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}+1)^{2}\kappa^{5}\|X_{\star}\|}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\phantom{\leq}+\frac{1}{64}\left(\frac{\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|}{\sigma_{\min}(\widetilde{S}_{t})}\right)^{2/3}.

(56b)

Here,
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}
can either be the Frobenius norm or the spectral norm, and
C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}>0
is given in Lemma
3
.

B.1
Proof of Lemma
13

We split the proof into three steps: (1) provide several useful approximation results regarding the
matrix inverses utilizing the facts that
\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|
and
\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|

are small (as shown by Lemma
3
); (2) proving the claims (
53a
),
(
54a
), (
54b
), and (
54f
) associated with
the signal term
S_{t+1}
; (3) proving the claims (
53b
),
(
54c
), (
54d
), (
54e
), and (
54g
) associated with
the noise term
N_{t+1}
. Note that our approximation results in step (1) include choices of some matrices
\{Q_{i}\}
with small spectral norms, whose choices may be different from lemma to lemma for simplicity of presentation;

B.1.1
Step 1: preliminaries

We know from (
22
) that the
overparametrization error
\widetilde{O}_{t}
is negligible compared
to the signals
\widetilde{S}_{t}
and
\sigma_{\min}(X_{\star})
. This combined with the decomposition (
19
) reveals
a desired approximation
(X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}\approx(V_{t}(\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{S}_{t}+\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t})V_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}
.
This approximation is formalized in the lemma below.

Lemma 16
.

If
\lambda\geq 4(\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|^{2}\vee 2\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\|\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|)
for some
t
, then

\displaystyle(X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\left(V_{t}(\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{S}_{t}+\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t})V_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\left(V_{t}(\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{S}_{t}+\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t})V_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I\right)^{-1}{E}^{\ref{prop:pre-approx0}.a}_{t}\left(V_{t}(\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{S}_{t}+\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t})V_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\left(V_{t}(\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{S}_{t}+\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t})V_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I\right)^{-1}\left(I+{E}^{\ref{prop:pre-approx0}.b}_{t}\right)

(57)

where the error terms
{E}^{\ref{prop:pre-approx0}.a}_{t}
,
{E}^{\ref{prop:pre-approx0}.b}_{t}
can be expressed as

\displaystyle{E}^{\ref{prop:pre-approx0}.a}_{t}

\displaystyle=(V_{{t,\perp}}\widetilde{O}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{O}_{t}V_{{t,\perp}}^{\top}+V_{t}\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{O}_{t}V_{{t,\perp}}^{\top}+V_{{t,\perp}}{\widetilde{O}_{t}}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t}V_{t}^{\top})Q_{1},

(58a)

\displaystyle{E}^{\ref{prop:pre-approx0}.b}_{t}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{-1}{E}^{\ref{prop:pre-approx0}.a}_{t}Q_{2},

(58b)

for some matrices
Q_{1},Q_{2}
such that
\max\{\|Q_{1}\|,\|Q_{2}\|\}\leq 2
.

Proof.

Expanding
X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}
according to (
19
), we have

X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}=V_{t}(\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{S}_{t}+\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t})V_{t}^{\top}+V_{{t,\perp}}\widetilde{O}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{O}_{t}V_{{t,\perp}}^{\top}+V_{t}\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{O}_{t}V_{{t,\perp}}^{\top}+V_{{t,\perp}}{\widetilde{O}_{t}}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t}V_{t}^{\top}.

The conclusion readily follows from Lemma
9
by setting therein
A=V_{t}(\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{S}_{t}+\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t})V_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I

and
B=V_{{t,\perp}}\widetilde{O}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{O}_{t}V_{{t,\perp}}^{\top}+V_{t}\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{O}_{t}V_{{t,\perp}}^{\top}+V_{{t,\perp}}{\widetilde{O}_{t}}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t}V_{t}^{\top}
, where the condition
\|A^{-1}B\|\leq 1/2
is satisfied since

\displaystyle\|A^{-1}B\|\leq\sigma_{\min}(A)^{-1}\|B\|\leq\lambda^{-1}\cdot(\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|^{2}+2\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\|)\leq 1/2.

∎

Moreover, the dominating term on the right hand side of (
57
) can be
equivalently written as

\displaystyle\left(V_{t}(\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{S}_{t}+\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t})V_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\Big(V_{t}(\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{S}_{t}+\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t}+\lambda I)V_{t}^{\top}+\lambda V_{{t,\perp}}V_{{t,\perp}}^{\top}\Big)^{-1}

\displaystyle=V_{t}(\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{S}_{t}+\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}V_{t}^{\top}+\lambda^{-1}V_{{t,\perp}}V_{{t,\perp}}^{\top}.

(59)

When the misalignment error
\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|
is small, we expect

(\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{S}_{t}+\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}\approx(\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{S}_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}
,
which is formalized in the following lemma that establishes
(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}+\lambda I)^{-1}\approx(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}
, due to the following approximation

\displaystyle(\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{S}_{t}+\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}+\lambda I)^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}

\displaystyle\approx\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}=(\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{S}_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}.

Lemma 17
.

If
\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|\leq\sigma_{\min}(X_{\star})/16
for some
t
,
then

\displaystyle(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}+\lambda I)^{-1}=(I+{E}^{\ref{prop:rearranged-inv-approx}}_{t})(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1},

(60)

where the error term
{E}^{\ref{prop:rearranged-inv-approx}}_{t}
is a matrix defined as

{E}^{\ref{prop:rearranged-inv-approx}}_{t}=\kappa^{2}\|X_{\star}\|^{-2}\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|Q_{1}(\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star})Q_{2},

(61)

where
Q_{1}
,
Q_{2}
are matrices of appropriate dimensions
satisfying
\|Q_{1}\|\leq 1
,
\|Q_{2}\|\leq 2
.
In particular, we have

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{E}^{\ref{prop:rearranged-inv-approx}}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\leq 2\kappa^{2}\|X_{\star}\|^{-2}\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|\cdot{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|},

(62)

where
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}
can be either the operator norm or the Frobenius norm.

Proof.

In order to apply Lemma
9
, setting
A=\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I
and
B=\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}
, it is straightforward to verify that

\|A^{-1}B\|=\|(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\|\leq\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\|^{2}\leq\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|^{2}\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\|^{2}\leq(1/16)^{2},

where we use the obvious fact that
\|(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\|\leq 1
.
Applying Lemma
9
, we obtain

\displaystyle(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}+\lambda I)^{-1}-(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}

\displaystyle=(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}Q(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}

\displaystyle=(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}(\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star})^{\top}(\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star})\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}Q(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}

for some matrix
Q
with
\|Q\|\leq 2
.
Since one may further write

\displaystyle(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}+\lambda I)^{-1}-(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\|^{2}\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\frac{\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}}{\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\|}\frac{(\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star})^{\top}}{\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|}(\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star})\frac{\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}}{\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\|}Q(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1},

the conclusion follows by setting
{E}^{\ref{prop:rearranged-inv-approx}}_{t}
as in (
61
) with

\displaystyle Q_{1}=(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\frac{\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}}{\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\|}\frac{(\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star})^{\top}}{\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|},\quad Q_{2}=\frac{\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}}{\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\|}Q.

The last inequality (
62
) is then a direct consequence of (
61
).
∎

B.1.2
Step 2: a key recursion

Recall the definition
\Delta_{t}
in (
48
), we can rewrite the update equation (
7
) as

X_{t+1}=X_{t}-\eta(X_{t}X_{t}^{\top}-M_{\star})X_{t}(X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}+\eta\Delta_{t}X_{t}(X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}.

(63)

Multiplying both sides of (
63
)
by
U_{\star}^{\top}
on the left, we obtain

\displaystyle S_{t+1}

\displaystyle=S_{t}-\eta S_{t}X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}(X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}+\eta\Sigma_{\star}^{2}S_{t}(X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}+\eta U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}X_{t}(X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}

\displaystyle=(1-\eta)S_{t}+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I+U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}U_{\star})S_{t}(X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}+\eta U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}U_{{\star,\perp}}N_{t}(X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}.

(64)

Similarly, multiplying both sides of (
63
) by
U_{{\star,\perp}}^{\top}
, we obtain

\displaystyle N_{t+1}

\displaystyle=N_{t}\left(I-\eta X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}(X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}\right)+\eta U_{{\star,\perp}}^{\top}\Delta_{t}X_{t}(X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}

\displaystyle=(1-\eta)N_{t}+\eta\lambda N_{t}(X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}+\eta U_{{\star,\perp}}^{\top}\Delta_{t}U_{\star}S_{t}(X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}+\eta U_{{\star,\perp}}^{\top}\Delta_{t}U_{{\star,\perp}}N_{t}(X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}.

(65)

These expressions motivate the need to study the terms
S_{t}(X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}
and
N_{t}(X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}
,
which we formalize in the following lemma.

Lemma 18
.

Under the same setting as Lemma
13
, we have

\displaystyle S_{t}(X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}

\displaystyle=(I+{E}^{\ref{prop:rearranged-inv-approx}}_{t})(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}V_{t}^{\top}+{E}^{\ref{lem:scaled-S-N-approx}.a}_{t},

(66a)

\displaystyle N_{t}(X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}(I+{E}^{\ref{prop:rearranged-inv-approx}}_{t})(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}V_{t}^{\top}+\lambda^{-1}\widetilde{O}_{t}V_{{t,\perp}}^{\top}+{E}^{\ref{lem:scaled-S-N-approx}.b}_{t},

(66b)

where
{E}^{\ref{prop:rearranged-inv-approx}}_{t}
is given in (
61
), and the error terms
{E}^{\ref{lem:scaled-S-N-approx}.a}_{t}
,
{E}^{\ref{lem:scaled-S-N-approx}.b}_{t}
can be expressed as

\displaystyle{E}^{\ref{lem:scaled-S-N-approx}.a}_{t}

\displaystyle=\kappa\lambda^{-1}\|X_{\star}\|^{-1}\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|Q_{1}(\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star})^{\top}Q_{2},

(67a)

\displaystyle{E}^{\ref{lem:scaled-S-N-approx}.b}_{t}

\displaystyle=\left(\widetilde{N}_{t}(\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{S}_{t}+\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}V_{t}^{\top}+\lambda^{-1}\widetilde{O}_{t}V_{{t,\perp}}^{\top}\right){E}^{\ref{prop:pre-approx0}.b}_{t}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{-1}(\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\|Q_{3}+\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|Q_{4}){E}^{\ref{prop:pre-approx0}.b}_{t}.

(67b)

for some matrices
\{Q_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq 4}
with spectral norm bounded by
2
, and
{E}^{\ref{prop:pre-approx0}.b}_{t}
defined in (
58b
).

Proof.

To begin, combining Lemma
16
and the discussion thereafter (cf. (
57
)–(
B.1.1
))
and the fact that
\widetilde{S}_{t}=S_{t}V_{t}
, we have
for some matrix
Q
with
\|Q\|\leq 2
that

\displaystyle S_{t}(X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{S}_{t}(\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{S}_{t}+\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}V_{t}^{\top}\left(I+{E}^{\ref{prop:pre-approx0}.b}_{t}\right)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{S}_{t}(\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{S}_{t}+\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}V_{t}^{\top}+\widetilde{S}_{t}(\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{S}_{t}+\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}\lambda^{-1}\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{O}_{t}Q

\displaystyle=(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}+\lambda I)^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}V_{t}^{\top}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\widetilde{S}_{t}(\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{S}_{t}+\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}(\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1})^{\top}(\widetilde{O}_{t}/\lambda)Q.

(68)

Note that the condition of Lemma
16
can be verified as follows: since

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.b}^{-C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.b}}\kappa^{-3}\cdot\|X_{\star}\|\cdot\sigma_{\min}\big((\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\big)\cdot\|\widetilde{S}_{t}\|\leq C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.b}^{-C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.b}}C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}\sigma_{\min}(X_{\star}),

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|\cdot\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\|\cdot\|\widetilde{S}_{t}\|\leq c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}\kappa^{-C_{\delta}/2}\|X_{\star}\|\cdot\frac{C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}\kappa\|X_{\star}\|}{\sigma_{\min}(X_{\star})}\leq c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}\sigma_{\min}(X_{\star})

provided
C_{\delta}\geq 6
, the bounds
c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}\lesssim c_{\delta}/c_{\lambda}^{3}
and
C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}\lesssim c_{\lambda}^{-1/2}
imply that when we choose
C_{\alpha}
to be large enough (depending on
c_{\lambda}
,
c_{\delta}
),

\displaystyle 2\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\|\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|\vee\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|^{2}\leq\lambda/4,

as desired.

Now the first term in (
68
) can be handled by invoking Lemma
17
, since its condition is verified by
\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|\leq c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}\kappa^{-(C_{\delta}/2-1)}\sigma_{\min}(X_{\star})\leq\sigma_{\min}(X_{\star})/16
provided
C_{\delta}\geq 2
and
c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}\leq 1/16
by choosing
c_{\delta}
sufficiently small (depending on
c_{\lambda}
). Namely,

\displaystyle(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}+\lambda I)^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}V_{t}^{\top}=(I+{E}^{\ref{prop:rearranged-inv-approx}}_{t})(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}V_{t}^{\top}.

For the second term, by noting that

\|\widetilde{S}_{t}(\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{S}_{t}+\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\|\leq\|\widetilde{S}_{t}(\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{S}_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\|\leq 1,

it can be expressed as

\lambda^{-1}\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|\widetilde{S}_{t}(\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{S}_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}(\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1})^{\top}(\widetilde{O}_{t}/\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|)Q=\kappa\lambda^{-1}\|X_{\star}\|^{-1}\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|Q_{1}(\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star})^{\top}Q_{2}

for
Q_{1}=\widetilde{S}_{t}(\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{S}_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\cdot\kappa^{-1}\|X_{\star}\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}
with
\|Q_{1}\|\leq 1
and

Q_{2}=(\widetilde{O}_{t}/\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|)Q
which satisfies
\|Q_{2}\|\leq\|Q\|\leq 2
.
Applying the above two bounds to (
68
)
yields (
66a
).

Similarly, moving to (
66b
), it follows that

\displaystyle N_{t}(X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}=

\displaystyle\left(\widetilde{N}_{t}(\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{S}_{t}+\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}V_{t}^{\top}+\lambda^{-1}\widetilde{O}_{t}V_{{t,\perp}}^{\top}\right)\left(I+{E}^{\ref{prop:pre-approx0}.b}_{t}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widetilde{N}_{t}(\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{S}_{t}+\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}V_{t}^{\top}+\lambda^{-1}\widetilde{O}_{t}V_{{t,\perp}}^{\top}+{E}^{\ref{lem:scaled-S-N-approx}.b}_{t},

(69)

where we have

\displaystyle{E}^{\ref{lem:scaled-S-N-approx}.b}_{t}

\displaystyle=\left(\widetilde{N}_{t}(\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{S}_{t}+\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}V_{t}^{\top}+\lambda^{-1}\widetilde{O}_{t}V_{{t,\perp}}^{\top}\right){E}^{\ref{prop:pre-approx0}.b}_{t}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{-1}(\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\|Q_{3}+\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|Q_{4}){E}^{\ref{prop:pre-approx0}.b}_{t}

for some matrices
Q_{3},Q_{4}
with
\|Q_{3}\|,\|Q_{4}\|\leq 1
.
In the last line we used
\|(\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{S}_{t}+\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}\|\leq\lambda^{-1}
. For the first term of (
69
), we use Lemma
17
and obtain

\displaystyle\widetilde{N}_{t}(\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{S}_{t}+\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}V_{t}^{\top}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{N}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}+\lambda I)^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}V_{t}^{\top}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}(I+{E}^{\ref{prop:rearranged-inv-approx}}_{t})(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}V_{t}^{\top}.

This yields the representation in (
66b
).

∎

B.1.3
Step 3: proofs associated with
S_{t+1}
.

With the help of Lemma
18
, we are ready to prove (
53a
) and
the associated norm bounds (
54a
), (
54b
), and (
54f
).
To begin with, we plug (
66a
), (
66b
)
into (
64
) and use
S_{t}=\widetilde{S}_{t}V_{t}^{\top}
to obtain

S_{t+1}=\left((1-\eta)I+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I+{E}^{a}_{t})(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\right)\widetilde{S}_{t}V_{t}^{\top}+\eta{E}^{b}_{t},

where the error terms
{E}^{a}_{t}
and
{E}^{b}_{t}
are

\displaystyle{E}^{a}_{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}U_{\star}+(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}U_{\star}+\lambda I){E}^{\ref{prop:rearranged-inv-approx}}_{t}+U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}U_{{\star,\perp}}\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}(I+{E}^{\ref{prop:rearranged-inv-approx}}_{t}),

\displaystyle{E}^{b}_{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}U_{\star}+\lambda I){E}^{\ref{lem:scaled-S-N-approx}.a}_{t}+U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}U_{{\star,\perp}}(\lambda^{-1}\widetilde{O}_{t}V_{{t,\perp}}^{\top}+{E}^{\ref{lem:scaled-S-N-approx}.b}_{t}).

This establishes the identity (
53a
).
To control
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{E}^{a}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}
, we observe that

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{E}^{a}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\leq{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\|\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}U_{\star}+\lambda I\|\cdot{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{E}^{\ref{prop:rearranged-inv-approx}}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\cdot\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|\cdot\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\|\cdot(1+\|{E}^{\ref{prop:rearranged-inv-approx}}_{t}\|)

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+c_{\ref{lem:Delta-bound}}\kappa^{-2C_{\delta}/3}+c_{\lambda}\right)\|X_{\star}\|^{2}\cdot{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{E}^{\ref{prop:rearranged-inv-approx}}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}\kappa^{-C_{\delta}/2}\|X_{\star}\|\cdot\sigma_{\min}^{-1}(X_{\star})\cdot(1+\|{E}^{\ref{prop:rearranged-inv-approx}}_{t}\|)\cdot{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\leq 2\|X_{\star}\|^{2}\cdot{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{E}^{\ref{prop:rearranged-inv-approx}}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\left(1+c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}(1+\|{E}^{\ref{prop:rearranged-inv-approx}}_{t}\|)\right){|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|},

where the second line follows from Lemma
12

and Equations (
12b
), (
22c
);
the last line holds since
c_{\ref{lem:Delta-bound}},c_{\lambda}
are sufficiently small and
C_{\delta}
is sufficiently large.
Now we invoke the bound (
62
) in Lemma
17

to see

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{E}^{\ref{prop:rearranged-inv-approx}}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\leq 2\kappa^{2}\|X_{\star}\|^{-2}\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\leq 2c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}\kappa^{2}\kappa^{-C_{\delta}/2}\|X_{\star}\|^{-1}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\leq 2c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}\kappa^{-4}\|X_{\star}\|^{-1}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|},

where the last line follows again by choosing sufficiently large
C_{\delta}\geq 12
.
Furthermore, since
\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|\leq c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}\kappa^{-C_{\delta}/2}\|X_{\star}\|
for small enough
c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}
, we obtain
\|{E}^{\ref{prop:rearranged-inv-approx}}_{t}\|\leq 1
. Combining these inequalities yields the claimed bound

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{E}^{a}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\leq 2c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}\kappa^{-4}\|X_{\star}\|\cdot{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+2{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}.

The bound of
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{E}^{b}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}
and
\|{E}^{b}_{t}\|
can be proved in a similar way,
utilizing the bound for
\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|
in (
24
).
In fact, a computation similar to the above shows

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{E}^{b}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\leq 2\|X_{\star}\|^{2}\cdot{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{E}^{\ref{lem:scaled-S-N-approx}.a}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\lambda^{-1}\|\Delta_{t}\|\cdot{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{O}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\|\Delta_{t}\|\cdot{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{E}^{\ref{lem:scaled-S-N-approx}.b}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\leq 2\kappa\lambda^{-1}\cdot\|X_{\star}\|\cdot\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|\cdot\|Q_{1}\|\cdot\|Q_{2}\|\cdot{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+100c_{\lambda}^{-1}\sigma_{\min}^{-1}(M_{\star})c_{\ref{lem:Delta-bound}}\kappa^{-2C_{\delta}/3}\|X_{\star}\|^{2}\cdot{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{O}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\phantom{\leq{}}+8\lambda^{-2}c_{\ref{lem:Delta-bound}}\kappa^{-2C_{\delta}/3}(\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\|+\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|)\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\|\cdot{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{O}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\leq 800\kappa^{3}c_{\lambda}^{-1}\|X_{\star}\|^{-1}\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|\cdot{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\frac{1}{48(C_{\max}+1)\kappa}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{O}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}.

Here,
C_{\max}
is the constant given by Lemma
3
. Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\|{E}^{b}_{t}\|\leq 800\kappa^{3}c_{\lambda}^{-1}\|X_{\star}\|^{-1}\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|\cdot\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|+\frac{1}{48(C_{\max}+1)\kappa}\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|.

The bound (
54f
) now follows directly from the bound of
\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|
in Lemma
3
, provided
c_{\delta}
is sufficiently small and
C_{\delta}
is sufficiently large.
To prove (
54b
), we note that

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|A|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\leq n\|A\|

(70)

for any unitarily invariant norm
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}
and real matrix
A\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times q}
with
p\vee q\leq n
(which can be easily verified
when
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}=\|\cdot\|
or
\|\cdot\|_{\fro}
).
Thus

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{E}^{b}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\leq\left(800\kappa^{3}c_{\lambda}^{-1}c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}\kappa^{-C_{\delta}/2}+\frac{1}{24(C_{\max}+1)\kappa}\right)n\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|\leq\left(\frac{\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|}{\sigma_{\min}(\widetilde{S}_{t})}\right)^{3/4}\sigma_{\min}(\widetilde{S}_{t})

where the last inequality follows from the control of
\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|

given by (
3
) provided
c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}
is sufficiently small
and
C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.b}
therein is sufficiently large. This establishes the first inequality in (
54b
), and the second inequality therein follows directly from (
3
).

B.1.4
Step 4: proofs associated with
\widetilde{N}_{t+1}
.

Now we move on to prove the identity (
53b
), and the norm
controls (
54c
), (
54d
), (
54e
), and (
54g
) associated with
the misalignment term
\widetilde{N}_{t+1}
.
Plugging (
66a
), (
66b
) into (
65
)
and using the decomposition
N_{t}=\widetilde{N}_{t}V_{t}^{\top}+\widetilde{O}_{t}V_{{t,\perp}}^{\top}
,
we have

\displaystyle N_{t+1}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\left((1-\eta)\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I+\eta{E}^{c}_{t}\right)(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}V_{t}^{\top}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\eta{E}^{e}_{t}(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}V_{t}^{\top}+\widetilde{O}_{t}V_{{t,\perp}}^{\top}+\eta{E}^{d}_{t},

where the error terms are defined to be

\displaystyle{E}^{c}_{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\lambda{E}^{\ref{prop:rearranged-inv-approx}}_{t},

\displaystyle{E}^{d}_{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq(\lambda I+U_{{\star,\perp}}^{\top}\Delta_{t}U_{{\star,\perp}}){E}^{\ref{lem:scaled-S-N-approx}.b}_{t}+\lambda^{-1}U_{{\star,\perp}}^{\top}\Delta_{t}U_{{\star,\perp}}\widetilde{O}_{t}V_{{t,\perp}}^{\top}+U_{{\star,\perp}}^{\top}\Delta_{t}U_{\star}{E}^{\ref{lem:scaled-S-N-approx}.a}_{t},

\displaystyle{E}^{e}_{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq U_{{\star,\perp}}^{\top}\Delta_{t}U_{\star}(I+{E}^{\ref{prop:rearranged-inv-approx}}_{t})+U_{{\star,\perp}}^{\top}\Delta_{t}U_{{\star,\perp}}\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}(I+{E}^{\ref{prop:rearranged-inv-approx}}_{t}).

This establishes the decomposition (
53b
).
The remaining norm controls follow from the expressions above and similar computation
as we have done for
S_{t+1}
. For the sake of brevity, we omit the details.

B.2
Proof of Lemma
14

Use the identity (
53a
) in Lemma
13
and the fact that
V_{t}
and
V_{{t,\perp}}
have orthogonal columns to obtain

\displaystyle S_{t+1}V_{t}+S_{t+1}V_{{t,\perp}}Q

\displaystyle=\left((1-\eta)I+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I+{E}^{a}_{t})(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\right)\widetilde{S}_{t}+\eta{E}^{b}_{t}(V_{t}+V_{{t,\perp}}Q)

\displaystyle=(I+\eta{E}^{\ref{lem:S-surrogate}}_{t})\left((1-\eta)I+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\right)\widetilde{S}_{t}

\displaystyle=(I+\eta{E}^{\ref{lem:S-surrogate}}_{t})\big((1-\eta)\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I+\eta\Sigma_{\star}^{2}\big)(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t},

(71)

where
{E}^{\ref{lem:S-surrogate}}_{t}
is defined to be

\displaystyle{E}^{\ref{lem:S-surrogate}}_{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left({E}^{a}_{t}(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}+{E}^{b}_{t}(V_{t}+V_{{t,\perp}}Q)\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\right)\left((1-\eta)I+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle={E}^{a}_{t}\left((1-\eta)(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+{E}^{b}_{t}(V_{t}+V_{{t,\perp}}Q)\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\left((1-\eta)I+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=:T_{1}+T_{2},

where the invertibility of
\widetilde{S}_{t}
follows from Lemma
3
,
and the invertibility of
(1-\eta)I+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}

follows from (
112
).

Since
(1-\eta)(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)\succeq\lambda I

and
\lambda\geq\frac{1}{100}c_{\lambda}\sigma_{\min}(M_{\star})
by (
12b
),
we have

\displaystyle\|T_{1}\|\leq\lambda^{-1}\|{E}^{a}_{t}\|\leq 100c_{\lambda}^{-1}\sigma_{\min}^{-1}(M_{\star})\|{E}^{a}_{t}\|.

In view of the bound (
54a
) on
\|{E}^{a}_{t}\|
in Lemma
13
,
we further have

\displaystyle\|T_{1}\|

\displaystyle\leq 100c_{\lambda}^{-1}\sigma_{\min}^{-2}(X_{\star})(\kappa^{-4}\|X_{\star}\|\cdot\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|+\|\Delta_{t}\|)

\displaystyle\leq 100c_{\lambda}^{-1}\kappa^{2}\|X_{\star}\|^{-2}(\kappa^{-4}c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}\kappa^{-C_{\delta}/2}+c_{\ref{lem:Delta-bound}}\kappa^{-2C_{\delta}/3})\|X_{\star}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{400(C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}+1)^{4}\kappa^{5}},

where the second inequality follows from (
22c
) in Lemma
3

and Lemma
12
,
and the last inequality holds as long as
c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}
and
c_{\ref{lem:Delta-bound}}
are sufficiently small
and
C_{\delta}
is sufficiently large (by first fixing
c_{\lambda}
and then choosing
c_{\delta}
to be sufficiently small).

The term
T_{2}
can be controlled in a similar way. Since
\|AB\|\leq\|A\|\cdot\|B\|
, one has

\displaystyle\|T_{2}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|{E}^{b}_{t}\|\cdot(\|V_{t}\|+\|V_{{t,\perp}}\|\|Q\|)\cdot\|\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\|\cdot\sigma_{\min}^{-1}\left((1-\eta)I+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{(i)}}}{{\leq}}3\|{E}^{b}_{t}\|\cdot\sigma_{\min}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t})\cdot\frac{\kappa}{1-\eta}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{(ii)}}}{{\leq}}6\kappa\left(\frac{\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|}{\sigma_{\min}(\widetilde{S}_{t})}\right)^{3/4}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{(iii)}}}{{\leq}}\frac{1}{400(C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}+1)^{4}\kappa^{5}}.

Here, (i) follows from the bound (
112
) and the facts that

\|V_{t}\|\vee\|V_{{t,\perp}}\|\leq 1
,
\|Q\|\leq 2
; (ii)
arises from the control (
54b
) on
\|{E}^{b}_{t}\|
in Lemma
13

as well as the condition
\eta\leq c_{\eta}\leq 1/2
;
and (iii) follows from the implication (
3
) of Lemma
3
.

The proof is completed by summing up the bounds on
\|T_{1}\|
and
\|T_{2}\|
.

B.3
Proof of Lemma
15

Similar to the proof of Lemma
14
, we can use the identity (
53b
) in Lemma
13

and the fact that
V_{t}
and
V_{{t,\perp}}
have orthogonal columns to obtain

\displaystyle N_{t+1}V_{t}+N_{t+1}V_{{t,\perp}}Q

\displaystyle=\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\big((1-\eta)\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I+\eta{E}^{c}_{t}\big)(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}+\eta{E}^{\ref{lem:NS-surrogate}.c}_{t}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}(I+\eta{E}^{\ref{lem:NS-surrogate}.a}_{t})\big((1-\eta)\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I\big)(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}+\eta{E}^{\ref{lem:NS-surrogate}.c}_{t},

(72)

where the error terms are defined to be

\displaystyle{E}^{\ref{lem:NS-surrogate}.c}_{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq{E}^{e}_{t}(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}+\eta^{-1}\widetilde{O}_{t}Q+{E}^{d}_{t}(V_{t}+V_{{t,\perp}}Q),

(73)

\displaystyle{E}^{\ref{lem:NS-surrogate}.a}_{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq{E}^{c}_{t}\big((1-\eta)\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I\big)^{-1}.

(74)

Combine (
72
) and (
71
) to arrive at

\displaystyle(N_{t+1}V_{t}+N_{t+1}V_{{t,\perp}}Q)(S_{t+1}V_{t}+S_{t+1}V_{{t,\perp}}Q)^{-1}

\displaystyle\quad=\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}(I+\eta{E}^{\ref{lem:NS-surrogate}.a}_{t})\big((1-\eta)\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I\big)\big((1-\eta)\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I+\eta\Sigma_{\star}^{2}\big)^{-1}(I+\eta{E}^{\ref{lem:S-surrogate}}_{t})^{-1}+\eta{E}^{\ref{lem:NS-surrogate}.b}_{t},

(75)

where, using

(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)\big((1-\eta)\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I+\eta\Sigma_{\star}^{2}\big)^{-1}=\big((1-\eta)I+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\big)^{-1},

we have

\displaystyle{E}^{\ref{lem:NS-surrogate}.b}_{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq{E}^{\ref{lem:NS-surrogate}.c}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)\big((1-\eta)\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I+\eta\Sigma_{\star}^{2}\big)^{-1}(I+\eta{E}^{\ref{lem:S-surrogate}}_{t})^{-1}

\displaystyle={E}^{e}_{t}\big((1-\eta)\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I+\eta\Sigma_{\star}^{2}\big)^{-1}(I+\eta{E}^{\ref{lem:S-surrogate}}_{t})^{-1}

\displaystyle\phantom{={}}+\eta^{-1}\widetilde{O}_{t}Q\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\left((1-\eta)I+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\right)^{-1}(I+\eta{E}^{\ref{lem:S-surrogate}}_{t})^{-1}

\displaystyle\phantom{={}}+{E}^{d}_{t}(V_{t}+V_{{t,\perp}}Q)\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\left((1-\eta)I+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\right)^{-1}(I+\eta{E}^{\ref{lem:S-surrogate}}_{t})^{-1}

\displaystyle\eqqcolon T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}.

It remains to bound
\|{E}^{\ref{lem:NS-surrogate}.a}\|

and
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{E}^{\ref{lem:NS-surrogate}.b}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}
.
By (
54c
), we have

\displaystyle\|{E}^{\ref{lem:NS-surrogate}.a}\|\leq\lambda^{-1}\|{E}^{c}_{t}\|

\displaystyle\leq 100c_{\lambda}^{-1}\sigma_{\min}^{-2}(X_{\star})\cdot\kappa^{-4}\|X_{\star}\|\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|

\displaystyle\leq 100c_{\lambda}^{-1}c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}\kappa^{-2}\kappa^{-C_{\delta}/2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{200(C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}+1)^{4}\kappa^{5}},

where the penultimate inequality follows from (
22c
)
and the last inequality holds with the proviso that
c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}
is sufficiently small
and
C_{\delta}
is sufficiently large.

Now we move to bound
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{E}^{\ref{lem:NS-surrogate}.b}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}
. To this end, the relation

\|(I+\eta{E}^{\ref{lem:S-surrogate}}_{t})^{-1}\|\leq 2
is quite helpful. This follows from
Lemma
14
in which we have established that
\|{E}^{\ref{lem:S-surrogate}}_{t}\|\leq 1/2
.
As a result of this relation, we obtain

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|T_{1}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\leq 2\lambda^{-1}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{E}^{e}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|},

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|T_{2}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\leq 2{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{O}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\cdot\|Q\|\cdot\|\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\|\cdot\left\|\left((1-\eta)I+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\right)^{-1}\right\|,

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|T_{3}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\leq 2{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{E}^{d}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\cdot(1+\|Q\|)\cdot\|\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\|\cdot\left\|\left((1-\eta)I+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\right)^{-1}\right\|.

Similar to the control of
T_{1}
in the proof of Lemma
14
, we can take the condition
\lambda\geq\frac{1}{100}c_{\lambda}\sigma_{\min}^{2}(X_{\star})
and the bound (
54e
) collectively
to see that

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|T_{1}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\leq 400c_{\lambda}^{-1}\kappa^{2}\|X_{\star}\|^{-2}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\frac{1}{64(C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}+1)^{2}\kappa^{3}\|X_{\star}\|}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}.

Regarding the terms
T_{2}
and
T_{3}
, we see from (
112
) that

\displaystyle\left\|\left((1-\eta)I+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\right)^{-1}\right\|\leq\frac{\kappa}{1-\eta}\leq 2\kappa,

as long
\eta
is sufficiently small.
Recalling the assumption
\|Q\|\leq 2
,
this allows us to obtain

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|T_{2}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\leq 8\eta^{-1}\kappa\frac{{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{O}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}}{\sigma_{\min}(\widetilde{S}_{t})}\leq 8\eta^{-1}\kappa n\frac{\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|}{\sigma_{\min}(\widetilde{S}_{t})},

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|T_{3}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\leq 12\kappa{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{E}^{d}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}/\sigma_{\min}(\widetilde{S}_{t}),

where the first inequality again uses the elementary fact
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{O}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\leq n\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|
in (
70
).

The desired bounds then follow from plugging in the bounds (
54d
)
and (
24
).

Appendix C
Proofs for Phase I

The goal of this section is to prove Lemma
3
in an inductive manner.
We achieve this goal in two steps. In Section
C.1
, we
find an
iteration number
t_{1}\leq T_{\min}/16
such that the claim (
22
)
is true at
t_{1}
. This establishes the base case. Then in Section
C.2
, we prove
the induction step, namely if the claim (
22
) holds for some iteration
t\geq t_{1}
,
we aim to show that (
22
)
continues to hold for the iteration
t+1
.
These two steps taken collectively finishes the proof of Lemma
3
.

C.1
Establishing the base case: Finding a valid
t_{1}

The following lemma ensures the existence of such an iteration number
t_{1}
.

Lemma 19
.

Under the same setting as Theorem
2
,
we have for some
t_{1}\leq T_{\min}/16
such that (
21
) holds
and that (
22
) hold with
t=t_{1}
.

The rest of this subsection is devoted to the proof of this lemma.

Define an auxiliary sequence

\displaystyle\widehat{X}_{t}\coloneqq\Big(I+\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})\Big)^{t}X_{0},

(76)

which can be viewed as power iterations on the matrix
\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})
from the initialization
X_{0}
.

In what follows, we first establish that the true iterates
\{X_{t}\}
stay close to the auxiliary iterates
\{\widehat{X}_{t}\}
as long as the initialization size
\alpha
is small; see Lemma
20
. This proximity then allows us to invoke the result in
Stöger and Soltanolkotabi, 2021
(see Lemma
21
) to establish Lemma
19
. For the rest of the appendices, we work on the following event given in (
18
):

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}=\{\|G\|\leq C_{G}\}\cap\{\sigma_{\min}^{-1}(\widehat{U}^{\top}G)\leq(2n)^{C_{G}}\}.

Step 1: controlling distance between
X_{t}
and
\widehat{X}_{t}
.

The following lemma guarantees the closeness between the two iterates
\{X_{t}\}
and
\{\widehat{X}_{t}\}
, with the proof deferred to Appendix
C.1.1
. Recall that
C_{G}
is the constant defined in the event
\mathcal{E}
in (
18
), and
c_{\lambda}
is the constant given in Theorem
2
.

Lemma 20
.

Suppose that
\lambda\geq\frac{1}{100}c_{\lambda}\sigma_{\min}^{2}(X_{\star})
. For any
\theta\in(0,1)
, there exists a large enough constant
K=K(\theta,c_{\lambda},C_{G})>0
such that the following holds: As long as
\alpha
obeys

\displaystyle\log\frac{\|X_{\star}\|}{\alpha}\geq\frac{K}{\eta}\log(2\kappa n)\cdot\Big(1+\log\Big(1+\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\|\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})\|\Big)\Big),

(77)

one has for all
t\leq\frac{1}{\theta\eta}\log(\kappa n)
:

\displaystyle\big\lVert X_{t}-\widehat{X}_{t}\big\rVert

\displaystyle\leq t\Big(1+\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\lVert\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})\rVert\Big)^{t}\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\|X_{\star}\|}.

(78)

Moreover,
\lVert X_{t}\rVert\leq\lVert X_{\star}\rVert
for all such
t
.

Step 2: borrowing a lemma from
Stöger and Soltanolkotabi, 2021
.

Compared to the original sequence
X_{t}
, the behavior of the power iterates
\widehat{X}_{t}
is much easier to analyze. Now that we have sufficient control over
\|X_{t}-\widehat{X}_{t}\|
, it is possible to show that
X_{t}
has the desired properties in Lemma
19
by first establishing the corresponding property of
\widehat{X}_{t}
and then invoking a standard matrix perturbation argument. Fortunately, such a strategy has been implemented by
Stöger and Soltanolkotabi, 2021
and wrapped into the following helper lemma.

Denote

\displaystyle s_{j}\coloneqq\sigma_{j}\Big(I+\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})\Big)=1+\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\sigma_{j}\big(\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})\big),\qquad j=1,2,\ldots,n

and recall that
\widehat{U}
(resp.
U_{\widetilde{X}_{t}}
) is an orthonormal basis of the eigenspace associated with the
r_{\star}
largest eigenvalues of
\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})
(resp.
\widetilde{X}_{t}
).

Lemma 21
.

There exists some small universal
c_{\ref{lem:Mahdi}}>0
such that the following hold.
Assume that for some
\gamma\leq c_{\ref{lem:Mahdi}}
,

\displaystyle\lVert(\id-\mathcal{A^{*}A})(M_{\star})\rVert\leq\gamma\sigma_{\min}^{2}(X_{\star}),

(79)

and furthermore,

\displaystyle\phi\coloneqq\frac{\alpha\|G\|s_{r_{\star}+1}^{t}+\|X_{t}-\widehat{X}_{t}\|}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{U}^{\top}G)s_{r_{\star}}^{t}}\leq c_{\ref{lem:Mahdi}}\kappa^{-2}.

(80)

Then there exists some universal
C_{\ref{lem:Mahdi}}>0
such that the following hold:

\displaystyle\sigma_{\min}(\widetilde{S}_{t})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\alpha}{4}\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{U}^{\top}G)s_{r_{\star}}^{t},

(81a)

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\ref{lem:Mahdi}}\phi\alpha\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{U}^{\top}G)s_{r_{\star}}^{t},

(81b)

\displaystyle\|U_{{\star,\perp}}^{\top}U_{\widetilde{X}_{t}}\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\ref{lem:Mahdi}}(\gamma+\phi),

(81c)

where
\widetilde{X}_{t}\coloneqq X_{t}V_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times r_{\star}}
.

Proof of Lemma
21
.

This follows from the claims of
Stöger and Soltanolkotabi, 2021
by noting that
\lVert\widetilde{O}_{t}\rVert=\lVert U_{{\star,\perp}}^{\top}X_{t}V_{t,\perp}\rVert\leq\lVert X_{t}V_{t,\perp}\rVert
for (
81b
).
3
3

3

The equation (31) in
Stöger and Soltanolkotabi, 2021
is stated in a weaker form than what they actually proved, and our (
81b
) indeed follows from the penultimate inequality in the proof of
Stöger and Soltanolkotabi, 2021
.

∎

Step 3: completing the proof.

Now, with the help of Lemma
21
, we are ready to prove Lemma
19
. We start with verifying the two assumptions in Lemma
21
.

Verifying assumption (
79
).

By the RIP in (
9
), Lemma
8
, and the condition of
\delta
in (
10
), we have

\displaystyle\big\lVert(\id-\mathcal{A^{*}A})(M_{\star})\big\rVert\leq\sqrt{r_{\star}}\delta\|M_{\star}\|\leq c_{\delta}\kappa^{-(C_{\delta}-2)}\sigma_{\min}^{2}(X_{\star})=:\gamma\sigma_{\min}^{2}(X_{\star}).

(82)

Here
\gamma=c_{\delta}\kappa^{-(C_{\delta}-2)}\leq c_{\ref{lem:Mahdi}}
, as

c_{\delta}
is assumed to be sufficiently small.

Verifying assumption (
80
).

By Weyl’s inequality and (
82
), we have

\displaystyle\Big|s_{j}-1-\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\sigma_{j}(M_{\star})\Big|\leq\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\big\lVert(\id-\mathcal{A^{*}A})(M_{\star})\big\rVert\leq\frac{\eta}{\lambda}c_{\delta}\kappa^{-(C_{\delta}-2)}\sigma_{\min}^{2}(X_{\star})\leq\frac{100c_{\delta}}{c_{\lambda}}\eta,

where the last inequality follows from the condition
\lambda\geq\frac{1}{100}c_{\lambda}\sigma_{\min}^{2}(X_{\star})
.
Furthermore, using the condition
\lambda\leq c_{\lambda}\sigma_{\min}^{2}(X_{\star})
assumed in (
12b
),
the above bound implies that, for some
C=C(c_{\lambda},c_{\delta})>0
,

\displaystyle s_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 1+\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\|M_{\star}\|+\frac{100c_{\delta}}{c_{\lambda}}\eta\leq 1+C\eta\kappa^{2},

(83a)

\displaystyle s_{r_{\star}}

\displaystyle\geq 1+\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\sigma_{\min}^{2}(X_{\star})-\frac{100c_{\delta}}{c_{\lambda}}\eta\geq 1+\frac{\eta}{2c_{\lambda}},

(83b)

\displaystyle s_{r_{\star}}

\displaystyle\leq 1+\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\sigma_{\min}^{2}(X_{\star})+\frac{100c_{\delta}}{c_{\lambda}}\eta\leq 1+\frac{2\eta}{c_{\lambda}},

(83c)

\displaystyle s_{r_{\star}+1}

\displaystyle\leq 1+\frac{100c_{\delta}}{c_{\lambda}}\eta\leq 1+\frac{\eta}{4c_{\lambda}},

(83d)

where we use the fact that
\sigma_{r_{\star}+1}(M_{\star})=0
, and
c_{\delta}\leq 1/400
.
Consequently we have
s_{r_{\star}}/s_{r_{\star}+1}\geq 1+c^{\prime}\eta

for some
c^{\prime}=c^{\prime}(c_{\lambda})>0
, assuming
c_{\eta}\leq c_{\lambda}
.
Thus for any large constant
L>0
,
there is some constant
c^{\prime\prime}=c^{\prime\prime}(c^{\prime})>0
such that,
setting
L^{\prime}=c^{\prime\prime}L\log(L)
we have

\displaystyle(s_{r_{\star}}/s_{r_{\star}+1})^{t}\geq(L\kappa n)^{L},\quad\forall t\geq\frac{L^{\prime}}{\eta}\log(\kappa n).

On the event
\mathcal{E}
given in (
18
),
we can choose
L
large enough so that
L\geq 2C_{G}
, hence
\|G\|\leq L

and
\sigma_{\min}^{-1}(\widehat{U}^{\top}G)\leq(2n)^{L/2}
.
Summarizing these inequalities, we see for
t\geq\frac{L^{\prime}}{\eta}\log(\kappa n)
,

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha\|G\|s_{r_{\star}+1}^{t}}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{U}^{\top}G)s_{r_{\star}}^{t}}

\displaystyle\leq L\sigma_{\min}^{-1}(\widehat{U}^{\top}G)(s_{r_{\star}+1}/s_{r_{\star}})^{t}

\displaystyle\leq L(2n)^{L/2}(L\kappa n)^{-L}\leq(L\kappa n)^{-L/2}.

(84)

Furthermore, invoking Lemma
20
with
\theta=1/(2L^{\prime})

(note that (
77
) is implied by the assumption (
12c
),
where
C_{\alpha}
is assumed sufficiently large,
considering
\lambda\geq\frac{1}{100}c_{\lambda}\sigma_{\min}^{2}(X_{\star})

and
\|\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})\|\leq\|M_{\star}\|+\gamma\sigma_{\min}^{2}(X_{\star})\leq 2\|X_{\star}\|^{2}

by (
82
)),
we obtain for any
t\leq\frac{1}{\theta\eta}\log(\kappa n)=\frac{2L^{\prime}}{\eta}\log(\kappa n)

that
\|X_{t}-\widehat{X}_{t}\|\leq ts_{1}^{t}\alpha^{2}/\lVert X_{\star}\rVert
. This implies

\displaystyle\frac{\|X_{t}-\widehat{X}_{t}\|}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}\big(\widehat{U}^{\top}G\big)s_{r_{\star}}^{t}}

\displaystyle\leq(s_{1}/s_{r_{\star}})^{t}\sigma_{\min}^{-1}\big(\widehat{U}^{\top}G\big)\alpha/\|X_{\star}\|

\displaystyle\leq s_{1}^{t}\sigma_{\min}^{-1}(\widehat{U}^{\top}G)\alpha/\|X_{\star}\|

\displaystyle\leq\exp(t\log(s_{1})+L\log(L\kappa n))\alpha/\|X_{\star}\|\leq(L\kappa n)^{-L/2}

(85)

where the second inequality follows from (
83b
),
the penultimate inequality follows from our choice of
L

which ensured
\sigma_{\min}^{-1}(\widehat{U}^{\top}G)\leq(2n)^{L/2}
,
and the last inequality follows from
(
83a
), our choice
t\leq\frac{2L^{\prime}}{\eta}\log(\kappa n)

and our assumption (
12c
) on
\alpha

which implies
\alpha/\|X_{\star}\|\leq(2\kappa n)^{-C_{\alpha}}
,
given that
C_{\alpha}
is sufficiently large,
e.g.
C_{\alpha}\geq C(L,c_{\lambda},c_{\eta})
.
It may also be inferred from the above arguments
that
L
can be made arbitrarily large by increasing
C_{\alpha}
.

Combining the above arguments, we conclude that
for any
t\in[(L^{\prime}/\eta)\log(\kappa n),(2L^{\prime}/\eta)\log(\kappa n)]
,
both of (
84
), (
85
) hold,
hence the condition in (
80
) can be verified by

\displaystyle\phi=\frac{\alpha\|G\|s_{r_{\star}+1}^{t}+\|X_{t}-\widehat{X}_{t}\|}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{U}^{\top}G)s_{r_{\star}}^{t}}

\displaystyle\leq 2(L\kappa n)^{-L/2}

(86)

\displaystyle\leq c_{\ref{lem:Mahdi}}\kappa^{-2},

by choosing
L
sufficiently large.

This completes the verification of both assumptions of Lemma
21
.
Upon noting that the upper threshold of
t
satisfies
(2L^{\prime}/\eta)\log(\kappa n)\leq T_{\min}/16
,
we will now invoke the conclusions of Lemma
21
to prove Lemma
19

for some
t\in[(L^{\prime}/\eta)\log(\kappa n),T_{\min}/16]
.

Proof of bound (
21
).

This can be inferred from (
81a
) in the following way.
Recalling that
\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{U}^{\top}G)\geq(2n)^{-C_{G}}
on the event
\mathcal{E}
,
and
s_{r_{\star}}\geq 1
by (
83b
),
we obtain from (
81a
) that

\displaystyle\sigma_{\min}(\widetilde{S}_{t_{1}})\geq\frac{1}{4}\alpha(2n)^{-C_{G}}\geq\alpha^{2}/\lVert X_{\star}\rVert,

given the condition (
12c
)
which guarantees

\frac{\alpha}{\|X_{\star}\|}\leq(2n)^{-C_{\alpha}/\eta}\leq\frac{1}{4}(2n)^{-C_{G}},

as long as
\eta\leq c_{\eta}\leq 1
and
C_{\alpha}\geq C_{G}+2
. The proof is complete.

Proof of bound (
22a
).

We combine (
81a
), (
81b
),
and (
86
) to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\lVert\widetilde{O}_{t_{1}}\rVert}{\sigma_{\min}(\widetilde{S}_{t_{1}})}\leq 4C_{\ref{lem:Mahdi}}\phi\leq 4C_{\ref{lem:Mahdi}}(L\kappa n)^{-L/2}\leq(L\kappa n/2)^{-L/2},

where the last inequality follows from taking
L
sufficiently large.
We further note that (
12b
) implies

\displaystyle\sigma_{\min}(\widetilde{S}_{t_{1}})\leq\|\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I\|^{1/2}\sigma_{\min}\left((\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\widetilde{S}_{t_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq(c_{\lambda}+1)^{1/2}\|X_{\star}\|\sigma_{\min}\left((\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\widetilde{S}_{t_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\|X_{\star}\|\sigma_{\min}\left((\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\widetilde{S}_{t_{1}}\right),

assuming
c_{\lambda}\leq 1
,
hence

\frac{\lVert\widetilde{O}_{t_{1}}\rVert}{\sigma_{\min}\left((\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\widetilde{S}_{t_{1}}\right)}\leq 2\|X_{\star}\|(L\kappa n/2)^{-L/2}\leq(C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.b}\kappa n)^{-C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.b}}\|X_{\star}\|,

as desired, with
C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.b}=L/4
as long as
L
is sufficiently large.
It is also clear that
C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.b}
can be made arbitrarily large by enlarging
C_{\alpha}

as
L
can be.

Proof of bound (
22b
).

We apply (
81b
) to yield

\displaystyle\lVert\widetilde{O}_{t_{1}}\rVert\leq C_{\ref{lem:Mahdi}}\phi\alpha\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{U}^{\top}G)s_{r_{\star}}^{t_{1}}\leq C_{G}C_{\ref{lem:Mahdi}}(L\kappa n)^{-L/2}\left(1+\frac{2\eta}{c_{\lambda}}\right)^{t_{1}}\alpha\leq\alpha^{5/6}\lVert X_{\star}\rVert^{1/6},

where the second inequality follows from
\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{U}^{\top}G)\leq\|G\|\leq C_{G}
by assumption
and from (
83c
);
the last inequality follows from
t_{1}\leq(2L^{\prime}/\eta)\log(\kappa n)

and from the condition (
12c
) on
\alpha
,
provided that
C_{\alpha}
is sufficiently large.

Proof of bound (
22c
).

We apply (
81c
) to yield that

\displaystyle\lVert U_{{\star,\perp}}^{\top}U_{\widetilde{X}_{t+1}}\rVert\leq C_{\ref{lem:Mahdi}}(\gamma+\phi)\leq\frac{c_{\delta}}{c_{\lambda}^{3}}\kappa^{-2C_{\delta}/3},

using the bounds of
\gamma
and
\phi
in (
82
) and (
86
),
provided that
c_{\lambda}^{3}\leq\frac{1}{2}\min(1,C_{\ref{lem:Mahdi}})

and
L\geq 2(C_{\delta}+1)
.
To further bound
\|\widetilde{N}_{t+1}\widetilde{S}_{t+1}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|
we need the following lemma.

Lemma 22
.

Assume
\widetilde{S}_{t}
is invertible, and
at least one of the following is true:
(i)
\|U_{{\star,\perp}}^{\top}U_{\widetilde{X}_{t}}\|\leq 1/4
;
(ii)
\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|\leq\kappa^{-1}\|X_{\star}\|/4
.
Then

\kappa^{-1}\|X_{\star}\|\|U_{{\star,\perp}}^{\top}U_{\widetilde{X}_{t}}\|\leq\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|\leq 2\|X_{\star}\|\|U_{{\star,\perp}}^{\top}U_{\widetilde{X}_{t}}\|.

The proof is postponed to Section
C.1.2
.
Returning to the proof of bound (
22c
),
the above lemma yields

\|\widetilde{N}_{t+1}\widetilde{S}_{t+1}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|\leq\frac{2c_{\delta}}{c_{\lambda}^{3}}\|X_{\star}\|\kappa^{-2C_{\delta}/3}\leq c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}\|X_{\star}\|\kappa^{-2C_{\delta}/3},

for some
c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}\lesssim c_{\delta}/c_{\lambda}^{3}
, as desired.

Proof of bound (
22d
).

We have

\displaystyle\lVert\widetilde{S}_{t_{1}}\rVert=\lVert U_{\star}^{\top}X_{t_{1}}V_{t_{1}}\rVert\leq\lVert X_{t_{1}}\rVert\leq\lVert X_{\star}\rVert,

where the last step follows from Lemma
20
.

C.1.1
Proof of Lemma
20

We prove the claim (
78
) by induction and also show that

\|X_{t}\|\leq\|X_{\star}\|
follows from (
78
). For the base case
t=0
, it holds by definition.
Assume that (
78
) holds for some
t\leq\frac{1}{\theta\eta}\log(\kappa n)-1
.
We aim to prove that (i)
\lVert X_{t}\rVert\leq\lVert X_{\star}\rVert

and that (ii) the inequality (
78
) continues to hold for
t+1
.

Proof of
\lVert X_{t}\rVert\leq\lVert X_{\star}\rVert
.

By the induction hypothesis we know

\big\lVert X_{t}-\widehat{X}_{t}\big\rVert\leq t\left(1+\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\lVert\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})\rVert\right)^{t}\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\|X_{\star}\|}.

In view of the constraint (
77
) on
\alpha
and the restriction
t\leq\frac{1}{\theta\eta}\log(\kappa n)
, we have

\displaystyle t\frac{\alpha}{\lVert X_{\star}\rVert}\leq\frac{1}{\theta\eta}\log(\kappa n)\cdot\frac{\eta}{K}\frac{1}{\log(\kappa n)}=\frac{1}{K\theta}\leq 1

as long as
K=K(\theta,c_{\lambda},C_{G})
is sufficiently large. This further implies

\|X_{t}-\widehat{X}_{t}\|\leq\left(t\frac{\alpha}{\|X_{\star}\|}\right)\left(1+\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\|\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})\|\right)^{t}\alpha\leq\left(1+\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\|\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})\|\right)^{t}\alpha.

On the other hand, since
\lVert X_{0}\rVert\leq C_{G}\alpha
under the event
\mathcal{E}
(cf. (
18
)), in view of (
76
), we have

\displaystyle\|\widehat{X}_{t}\|\leq\Big(1+\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\|\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})\|\Big)^{t}\|X_{0}\|\leq C_{G}\Big(1+\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\|\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})\|\Big)^{t}\alpha.

Thus for a large enough
K=K(\theta,c_{\lambda},C_{G})
, we have

\displaystyle\|X_{t}\|\leq\|X_{t}-\widehat{X}_{t}\|+\|\widehat{X}_{t}\|\leq\Big(1+\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\|\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})\|\Big)^{t}(C_{G}+1)\alpha\leq\sqrt{c_{\lambda}/200}\cdot\kappa^{-1}\|X_{\star}\|,

(87)

where the last inequality follows from the condition on
t
and the choice of
\alpha
in (
77
):

\displaystyle\log\frac{\lVert X_{\star}\rVert}{\alpha}\geq\log\frac{\sqrt{200}(C_{G}+1)\kappa}{\sqrt{c_{\lambda}}}+t\log\Big(1+\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\|\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})\|\Big).

The inequality (
87
) clearly implies
\lVert X_{t}\rVert\leq\lVert X_{\star}\rVert
.

Proof of (
78
) at the induction step.

The proof builds on a key recursive relation on
\big\lVert X_{t+1}-\widehat{X}_{t+1}\big\rVert
, from which the induction follows readily from our assumption.

Step 1: building a recursive relation on
\big\lVert X_{t+1}-\widehat{X}_{t+1}\big\rVert
.

By definition (
76
), we have
\widehat{X}_{t+1}=\big(I+\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})\big)\widehat{X}_{t}
, which implies the following decomposition:

\displaystyle X_{t+1}-\widehat{X}_{t+1}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\Big[X_{t+1}-\Big(I+\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})\Big)X_{t}\Big]}_{\eqqcolon T_{1}}+\underbrace{\Big(I+\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})\Big)(X_{t}-\widehat{X}_{t})}_{\eqqcolon T_{2}}.

(88)

We shall control each term separately.

•

The second term
T_{2}
can be trivially bounded as

\|T_{2}\|=\bigg\lVert\Big(I+\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})\Big)(X_{t}-\widehat{X}_{t})\bigg\rVert\leq\Big(1+\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\lVert\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})\rVert\Big)\big\lVert X_{t}-\widehat{X}_{t}\big\rVert.

(89)

•

Turning to the first term
T_{1}
,
by the update rule (
7
) of
X_{t+1}
and the triangle inequality, we further have

\displaystyle\|T_{1}\|=\left\|X_{t+1}-\Big(I+\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})\Big)X_{t}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\eta\mathcal{A^{*}A}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\top})X_{t}(X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}\right\|

\displaystyle\phantom{\leq{}}+\left\|\eta\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})X_{t}\big((X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}-\lambda^{-1}I\big)\right\|.

(90)

Since
\|(X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}\|\leq\lambda^{-1}
,
it follows that the first term in (
•
‣
C.1.1
) can be bounded by

\left\|\eta\mathcal{A^{*}A}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\top})X_{t}(X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}\right\|\leq\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\|\mathcal{A^{*}A}(X_{t}^{\top}X_{t})\|\|X_{t}\|.

In addition, since
\sqrt{c_{\lambda}/200}\cdot\kappa^{-1}\|X_{\star}\|=\sqrt{c_{\lambda}\sigma_{\min}^{2}(X_{\star})/200}\leq\sqrt{\lambda/2}

by the condition
\lambda\geq\frac{1}{100}c_{\lambda}\sigma_{\min}^{2}(X_{\star})
, we have by (
87
) that
\lVert X_{t}\rVert\leq\sqrt{\lambda/2}
. Therefore, invoking Lemma
9

implies that

(X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}-\lambda^{-1}I=\lambda^{-2}X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}Q,\quad\text{for some }Q\;\text{with }\lVert Q\rVert\leq 2.

As a result, the second term in (
•
‣
C.1.1
) can be bounded by

\left\|\eta\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})X_{t}\big((X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}-\lambda^{-1}I\big)\right\|\leq 2\frac{\eta}{\lambda^{2}}\|\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})\|\|X_{t}\|^{3}.

Combining the above two inequalities leads to

\left\|T_{1}\right\|\leq\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\left(\|\mathcal{A^{*}A}(X_{t}^{\top}X_{t})\|+\frac{2}{\lambda}\|\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})\|\|X_{t}\|^{2}\right)\|X_{t}\|.

In view of Lemma
8
,
we know
\lVert\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})\rVert\lesssim r_{\star}\lVert M_{\star}\rVert

and
\lVert\mathcal{A^{*}A}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\top})\rVert\lesssim r\lVert X_{t}\rVert^{2}
. Plugging these relations into the previous bound leads to

\|T_{1}\|\lesssim\frac{\eta r}{\lambda}\Big(1+\frac{\lVert M_{\star}\rVert}{\lambda}\Big)\lVert X_{t}\rVert^{3}\lesssim\frac{\eta\kappa^{2}r}{\|M_{\star}\|}\kappa^{2}\|X_{t}\|^{3},

(91)

where the last inequality follows from

\lambda\gtrsim\sigma_{\min}^{2}(X_{\star})=\kappa^{-2}\|M_{\star}\|
(cf. (
12b
)).

Putting the bounds on
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
together leads to

\big\lVert X_{t+1}-\widehat{X}_{t+1}\big\rVert\leq\Big(1+\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\|\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})\|\Big)\big\lVert X_{t}-\widehat{X}_{t}\big\rVert+\frac{C\eta\kappa^{4}r}{\|M_{\star}\|}\|X_{t}\|^{3}

(92)

for some universal constant
C=C(c_{\lambda})>0
.

Step 2: finishing the induction.

By the bound of
\|X_{t}\|
in (
87
), it suffices to prove

\displaystyle t\Big(1+\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\lVert\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})\rVert\Big)^{t+1}\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\|X_{\star}\|}+\frac{C(C_{G}+1)^{3}\eta\kappa^{4}r}{\|X_{\star}\|^{2}}\Big(1+\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\lVert\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})\rVert\Big)^{3t}\alpha^{3}

\displaystyle\leq(t+1)\Big(1+\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\lVert\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})\rVert\Big)^{t+1}\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\|X_{\star}\|}.

This is equivalent to

\displaystyle C(C_{G}+1)^{3}\eta\kappa^{4}r\left(1+\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\|\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})\|\right)^{2t-1}\leq\frac{\|X_{\star}\|}{\alpha},

which again follows readily from our assumption
t\leq\frac{1}{\theta\eta}\log(\kappa n)

and the assumption (
77
) on
\alpha
which implies

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{\|X_{\star}\|}{\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle\geq(2t-1)\log\left(1+\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\|\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})\|\right)+4\log\kappa+\log n+K

\displaystyle\geq(2t-1)\log\left(1+\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\|\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})\|\right)+\log(\eta\kappa^{4}r)+\log(C(C_{G}+1)^{3})

provided
K=K(\theta,c_{\lambda},C_{G})
is sufficiently large. The proof is complete.

C.1.2
Proof of Lemma
22

We begin with the following observation:

\displaystyle\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}

\displaystyle=U_{{\star,\perp}}^{\top}U_{\widetilde{X}_{t}}\Sigma_{\widetilde{X}_{t}}V_{\widetilde{X}_{t}}^{\top}V_{\widetilde{X}_{t}}\Sigma_{\widetilde{X}_{t}}^{-1}(U_{\star}^{\top}U_{\widetilde{X}_{t}})^{-1}

\displaystyle=U_{{\star,\perp}}^{\top}U_{\widetilde{X}_{t}}(U_{\star}^{\top}U_{\widetilde{X}_{t}})^{-1}

(93)

where we use: (i)
\widetilde{N}_{t}=U_{{\star,\perp}}^{\top}(U_{\widetilde{X}_{t}}\Sigma_{\widetilde{X}_{t}}V_{\widetilde{X}_{t}}^{\top})
and
\widetilde{S}_{t}=U_{\star}^{\top}U_{\widetilde{X}_{t}}\Sigma_{\widetilde{X}_{t}}V_{\widetilde{X}_{t}}^{\top}
; (ii)
\widetilde{X}_{t}
is invertible since
\widetilde{S}_{t}
is invertible, and hence
V_{\widetilde{X}_{t}}
has rank
r_{\star}
and
\Sigma_{\widetilde{X}_{t}},U_{\star}^{\top}U_{\widetilde{X}_{t}}
are also invertible.
We will show that the above quantity is small if (and only if)

U_{{\star,\perp}}^{\top}U_{\widetilde{X}_{t}}
is small.

Turning to the proof, we first show that (ii) implies (i),
thus it suffices to prove the lemma under the condition (i).
In fact, in virtue of (
93
) we have

\|U_{{\star,\perp}}^{\top}U_{\widetilde{X}_{t}}\|\leq\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\|\|U_{\star}^{\top}U_{\widetilde{X}_{t}}\|\leq\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\|\leq\sigma_{\min}(X_{\star})^{-1}\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|,

where we used
\|U_{\star}^{\top}U_{\widetilde{X}_{t}}\|\leq\|U_{\star}\|\|U_{\widetilde{X}_{t}}\|\leq 1
.
Consequently,
\|U_{{\star,\perp}}^{\top}U_{\widetilde{X}_{t}}\|\leq 1/4

if
\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|\leq\kappa^{-1}\|X_{\star}\|/4
, as claimed.

We proceed to show that the conclusion holds assuming condition (i).
The first inequality has already been established above.
For the second inequality, using (
93
) again,
it suffices to prove
\|(U_{\star}^{\top}U_{\widetilde{X}_{t}})^{-1}\|\leq 2
,
which is in turn equivalent to

\sigma_{\min}(U_{\star}^{\top}U_{\widetilde{X}_{t}})\geq 1/2
.
Now note that

U_{\widetilde{X}_{t}}=U_{\star}U_{\star}^{\top}U_{\widetilde{X}_{t}}+U_{{\star,\perp}}U_{{\star,\perp}}^{\top}U_{\widetilde{X}_{t}}
,
thus

\displaystyle\sigma_{\min}(U_{\star}^{\top}U_{\widetilde{X}_{t}})

\displaystyle=\sigma_{r_{\star}}(U_{\star}^{\top}U_{\widetilde{X}_{t}})

\displaystyle\geq\sigma_{r_{\star}}(U_{\star}U_{\star}^{\top}U_{\widetilde{X}_{t}})

\displaystyle\geq\sigma_{r_{\star}}(U_{\widetilde{X}_{t}})-\|U_{{\star,\perp}}U_{{\star,\perp}}^{\top}U_{\widetilde{X}_{t}}\|

\displaystyle\geq 1-\|U_{{\star,\perp}}^{\top}U_{\widetilde{X}_{t}}\|\geq 3/4.

In the last line, we used
\sigma_{r_{\star}}(U_{\widetilde{X}_{t}})=1
,
which follows from
U_{\widetilde{X}_{t}}
being a
n\times r_{\star}
orthonormal matrix,
and the assumption (i).
This completes the proof.

C.2
Establishing the induction step

The claimed invertibility of
\widetilde{S}_{t}
follows from induction and from Lemma
4
.
In fact, by (
21
) we know
\widetilde{S}_{t_{1}}
is invertible,
and by Lemma
4
we know that if
\widetilde{S}_{t}
is invertible,

\widetilde{S}_{t+1}
would also be invertible since
\widetilde{S}_{t}
(resp.
\widetilde{S}_{t+1}
)
has the same invertibility as
(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}

(resp.
(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t+1}
).
For the rest of the proof we focus on establishing (
22
) by induction.

For the induction step we need to understand the one-step behaviors of

\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|
,
\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|
, and
\|\widetilde{S}_{t}\|
, which are supplied by
the following lemmas.

Lemma 23
.

For any
t
such that (
22
) holds,

\|\widetilde{O}_{t+1}\|\leq\left(1+\frac{1}{12C_{\max}\kappa}\eta\right)\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|.

(94)

Lemma 24
.

For any
t
such that (
22
) holds,
setting
Z_{t}=\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}
,
there exists some universal constant
C_{\ref{lem:misalign-update}}>0
such that

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t+1}\widetilde{S}_{t+1}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\leq\left(1-\frac{\eta}{3(\|Z_{t}\|+\eta)}\right){|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\eta\frac{C_{\ref{lem:misalign-update}}\kappa^{2}}{c_{\lambda}\|X_{\star}\|}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\eta\left(\frac{\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|}{\sigma_{\min}(\widetilde{S}_{t})}\right)^{1/2}\|X_{\star}\|.

(95)

In particular, if
c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}=100C_{\ref{lem:misalign-update}}(C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}+1)^{4}c_{\delta}/c_{\lambda}
, then

\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|\leq c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}\kappa^{-C_{\delta}/2}\|X_{\star}\|
implies

\|\widetilde{N}_{t+1}\widetilde{S}_{t+1}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|\leq c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}\kappa^{-C_{\delta}/2}\|X_{\star}\|
.

Lemma 25
.

For any
t
such that (
22
) holds,

\|\widetilde{S}_{t+1}\|\leq\left(1-\frac{\eta}{2}\right)\|\widetilde{S}_{t}\|+100c_{\lambda}^{-1/2}\eta\kappa\|X_{\star}\|.

(96)

In particular, if
C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}=200c_{\lambda}^{-1/2}
,
then
\|\widetilde{S}_{t}\|\leq C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}\kappa\|X_{\star}\|
implies
\|\widetilde{S}_{t+1}\|\leq C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}\kappa\|X_{\star}\|
.

We now return to the induction step.
Recall that we need to show
(
22a
)–(
22d
) hold for
t+1
.
It is obvious that (
22b
)–(
22d
)
hold for
t+1
by the induction hypothesis and the above lemmas.
It remains to prove (
22a
).
To this end we distinguish two cases:

\sigma_{\min}((\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\widetilde{S}_{t})\leq 1/3

and
\sigma_{\min}((\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\widetilde{S}_{t})>1/3
.
In the former case, (
22a
) for
t+1
follows from
Lemma
23
and Lemma
4

(to be proved in Appendix
D.1
),
which imply (provided
C_{\max}\geq 2
)

\frac{\|\widetilde{O}_{t+1}\|}{\sigma_{\min}((\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\widetilde{S}_{t+1})}\leq\frac{\left(1+\frac{\eta}{4C_{\max}\kappa}\right)}{(1+\eta/8)}\frac{\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|}{\sigma_{\min}((\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\widetilde{S}_{t})}\leq\frac{\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|}{\sigma_{\min}((\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\widetilde{S}_{t})},

as desired. In the latter case where

\sigma_{\min}((\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\widetilde{S}_{t})>1/3
,
one may apply the first part of Lemma
4

to deduce that
\sigma_{\min}((\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\widetilde{S}_{t+1})\geq 1/10

(given that
\eta\leq c_{\eta}
for some sufficiently small constant
c_{\eta}
).
This combined with (
22b
) for
t+1
(already proved)
yields desired inequality (
22a
) for
t+1
,
given our assumption (
12c
) on the smallness of
\alpha
.
This completes the proof.

C.2.1
Proof of Lemma
23

If
r=r_{\star}
, then we have
\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|=0
for all
t\geq 0
.
The conclusion follows trivially.
Therefore, we only consider the case when
r>r_{\star}
.
By definition, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{O}_{t+1}=N_{t+1}V_{{t+1,\perp}}

\displaystyle=N_{t+1}V_{t}V_{t}^{\top}V_{{t+1,\perp}}+N_{t+1}V_{{t,\perp}}V_{{t,\perp}}^{\top}V_{{t+1,\perp}}

\displaystyle=-N_{t+1}V_{t}(S_{t+1}V_{t})^{-1}S_{t+1}V_{{t,\perp}}V_{{t,\perp}}^{\top}V_{{t+1,\perp}}+N_{t+1}V_{{t,\perp}}V_{{t,\perp}}^{\top}V_{{t+1,\perp}},

where the last inequality uses the fact that

V_{t}^{\top}V_{{t+1,\perp}}=-(S_{t+1}V_{t})^{-1}S_{t+1}V_{{t,\perp}}V_{{t,\perp}}^{\top}V_{{t+1,\perp}}
.
To see this, note that

S_{t+1}V_{{t+1,\perp}}=0\qquad\Longrightarrow\qquad S_{t+1}V_{t}V_{t}^{\top}V_{{t+1,\perp}}=-S_{t+1}V_{{t,\perp}}V_{{t,\perp}}^{\top}V_{{t+1,\perp}}.

Left-multiplying both sides by
(S_{t+1}V_{t})^{-1}
yields the desired identity. Note that the invertibility of
S_{t+1}V_{t}
follows from the invertibility of
\widetilde{S}_{t}
by inserting
Q=0
in Lemma
14
.

By Lemma
13
, we immediately
obtain that
S_{t+1}V_{{t,\perp}}=\eta{E}^{b}_{t}V_{{t,\perp}}
,
and
N_{t+1}V_{{t,\perp}}=\widetilde{O}_{t}+\eta{E}^{d}_{t}V_{{t,\perp}}
,
where
\|{E}^{b}_{t}\|\vee\|{E}^{d}_{t}\|\leq\frac{1}{24C_{\max}\kappa}\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|
.
Assume for now that

\|N_{t+1}V_{t}(S_{t+1}V_{t})^{-1}\|\leq 1.

(97)

In addition, notice that
\|V_{{t,\perp}}^{\top}V_{{t+1,\perp}}\|\leq 1
since both factors are orthonormal matrices, we have

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{O}_{t+1}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|+\eta\|N_{t+1}V_{t}(S_{t+1}V_{t})^{-1}\|\|{E}^{b}_{t}\|+\eta\|{E}^{d}_{t}\|

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\frac{1}{12C_{\max}\kappa}\eta\right)\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|,

as desired. It remains to prove (
97
).

Proof of bound (
97
).

This can be done by plugging
Q=0
into Lemma
15
and bounding the resulting expression.
This (in fact, a much stronger inequality) will be done in detail in the proof of Lemma
24
, to be presented soon in Section
C.2.2
.
In fact, the resulting expression is the same as (
102
) there (albeit with different values of
{E}^{\ref{lem:S-surrogate}.a}_{t}
,
{E}^{\ref{lem:NS-surrogate}.a}_{t}
,
{E}^{\ref{lem:NS-surrogate}.b}_{t}
,
which do not affect the proof).
Following the same strategy to control (
102
) there, we may show that
\|N_{t+1}V_{t}(S_{t+1}V_{t})^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|
enjoys the same bound (
107
) as
\|\widetilde{N}_{t+1}\widetilde{S}_{t+1}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|
,
the right hand side of which is less than
\kappa^{-1}\|X_{\star}\|=\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\|^{-1}
given (
22c
) and (
22d
).
Thus
\|N_{t+1}V_{t}(S_{t+1}V_{t})^{-1}\|\leq\|N_{t+1}V_{t}(S_{t+1}V_{t})^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\|\leq 1
as claimed.

C.2.2
Proof of Lemma
24

Denoting
\widetilde{X}_{t}\coloneqq X_{t}V_{t}
, we have
\widetilde{N}_{t}=U_{{\star,\perp}}^{\top}\widetilde{X}_{t}
and
\widetilde{S}_{t}=U_{\star}^{\top}\widetilde{X}_{t}
.
Suppose for the moment that

\displaystyle\|(V_{t}^{\top}V_{t+1})^{-1}\|\leq 2,

(98)

whose proof is deferred to the end of this section.
We can write the update equation of
\widetilde{X}_{t}
as

\displaystyle\widetilde{X}_{t+1}

\displaystyle=X_{t+1}V_{t+1}=X_{t+1}V_{t}V_{t}^{\top}V_{t+1}+X_{t+1}V_{{t,\perp}}V_{{t,\perp}}^{\top}V_{t+1}

\displaystyle=\left(X_{t+1}V_{t}+X_{t+1}V_{{t,\perp}}V_{{t,\perp}}^{\top}V_{t+1}(V_{t}^{\top}V_{t+1})^{-1}\right)V_{t}^{\top}V_{t+1}.

(99)

Left-multiplying both sides of
(
99
) with
U_{{\star,\perp}}
(or
U_{\star}
),
we obtain

\displaystyle\widetilde{N}_{t+1}

\displaystyle=(N_{t+1}V_{t}+N_{t+1}V_{{t,\perp}}Q)V_{t}^{\top}V_{t+1},

(100a)

\displaystyle\widetilde{S}_{t+1}

\displaystyle=(S_{t+1}V_{t}+S_{t+1}V_{{t,\perp}}Q)V_{t}^{\top}V_{t+1},

(100b)

where we define
Q\coloneqq V_{{t,\perp}}^{\top}V_{t+1}(V_{t}^{\top}V_{t+1})^{-1}
.
