Consequently, we arrive at

\widetilde{N}_{t+1}\widetilde{S}_{t+1}^{-1}=(N_{t+1}V_{t}+N_{t+1}V_{{t,\perp}}Q)(S_{t+1}V_{t}+S_{t+1}V_{{t,\perp}}Q)^{-1}.

(101)

Since
\|Q\|\leq 2
(which is an immediate implication of (
98
)),
we can invoke Lemma
15
to obtain

\displaystyle\widetilde{N}_{t+1}\widetilde{S}_{t+1}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}=

\displaystyle\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}(I+\eta{E}^{\ref{lem:NS-surrogate}.a}_{t})A_{t}(A_{t}+\eta\Sigma_{\star}^{2})^{-1}(I+\eta{E}^{\ref{lem:S-surrogate}}_{t})^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}+\eta{E}^{\ref{lem:NS-surrogate}.b}_{t}\Sigma_{\star}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}(I+\eta\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}{E}^{\ref{lem:NS-surrogate}.a}_{t}\Sigma_{\star})H_{t}(H_{t}+\eta I)^{-1}(I+\eta\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}{E}^{\ref{lem:S-surrogate}}_{t}\Sigma_{\star})^{-1}+\eta{E}^{\ref{lem:NS-surrogate}.b}_{t}\Sigma_{\star},

(102)

where for simplicity of notation, we denote

\displaystyle A_{t}\coloneqq(1-\eta)\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I,\quad\text{and}\quad H_{t}\coloneqq\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}A_{t}\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}.

In addition, we have

\displaystyle\|{E}^{\ref{lem:S-surrogate}}_{t}\|+\|{E}^{\ref{lem:NS-surrogate}.a}_{t}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{64\kappa^{5}},

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{E}^{\ref{lem:NS-surrogate}.b}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\leq 800c_{\lambda}^{-1}\kappa^{2}\|X_{\star}\|^{-2}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\frac{1}{64(C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}+1)^{2}\kappa^{5}\|X_{\star}\|}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\frac{1}{64}\left(\frac{\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|}{\sigma_{\min}(\widetilde{S}_{t})}\right)^{2/3}.

Moreover, it is clear that
\eta\leq c_{\eta}\leq 1\leq\kappa^{4}
since
\kappa\geq 1
,
and that
\|H_{t}\|\leq\kappa^{2}(1+\|\widetilde{S}_{t}\|^{2}/\|X_{\star}\|^{2})\leq(C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}+1)^{2}\kappa^{4}
.
Hence we have

\|H_{t}\|+\eta\leq 2(C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}+1)^{2}\kappa^{4}

which implies

\|{E}^{\ref{lem:S-surrogate}}_{t}\|+\|{E}^{\ref{lem:NS-surrogate}.a}_{t}\|\leq\frac{1}{24\kappa}\frac{1}{\|H_{t}\|+\eta}.

(103)

Similarly we may also show

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{E}^{\ref{lem:NS-surrogate}.b}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\leq 800c_{\lambda}^{-1}\kappa^{2}\|X_{\star}\|^{-2}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\frac{1}{12(\|H_{t}\|+\eta)\|X_{\star}\|}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|}{\sigma_{\min}(\widetilde{S}_{t})}\right)^{2/3}.

(104)

Since
H_{t}
is obviously positive definite, we have

\|H_{t}(H_{t}+\eta I)^{-1}\|\leq 1-\frac{\eta}{\|H_{t}\|+\eta}.

(105)

Thus

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t+1}\widetilde{S}_{t+1}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\leq

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{\eta}{\|H_{t}\|+\eta}\right)(1-\eta\kappa\|{E}^{\ref{lem:S-surrogate}}_{t}\|)^{-1}(1+\eta\kappa\|{E}^{\ref{lem:NS-surrogate}.a}_{t}\|){|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\eta{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{E}^{\ref{lem:NS-surrogate}.b}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\|X_{\star}\|.

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{\eta}{\|H_{t}\|+\eta}\right)\left(1+\frac{1}{12}\frac{\eta}{\|H_{t}\|+\eta}\right)^{2}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle+\eta\frac{800\kappa^{2}}{c_{\lambda}\|X_{\star}\|}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\frac{1}{12}\frac{\eta}{\|H_{t}\|+\eta}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\frac{1}{2}\eta\left(\frac{\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|}{\sigma_{\min}(\widetilde{S}_{t})}\right)^{2/3}\|X_{\star}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{5}{6}\frac{\eta}{\|H_{t}\|+\eta}\right){|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\frac{1}{12}\frac{\eta}{\|H_{t}\|+\eta}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle+\eta\frac{800\kappa^{2}}{c_{\lambda}\|X_{\star}\|}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\frac{1}{2}\eta\left(\frac{\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|}{\sigma_{\min}(\widetilde{S}_{t})}\right)^{2/3}\|X_{\star}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{3}{4}\frac{\eta}{\|H_{t}\|+\eta}\right){|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\eta\frac{800\kappa^{2}}{c_{\lambda}\|X_{\star}\|}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\frac{1}{2}\eta\left(\frac{\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|}{\sigma_{\min}(\widetilde{S}_{t})}\right)^{2/3}\|X_{\star}\|\

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{3}{4}\frac{\eta}{\|Z_{t}\|+\eta}\right){|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\eta\frac{800\kappa^{2}}{c_{\lambda}\|X_{\star}\|}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\frac{1}{2}\eta\left(\frac{\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|}{\sigma_{\min}(\widetilde{S}_{t})}\right)^{2/3}\|X_{\star}\|,

(106)

where in the second inequality we used
(1-x)^{-1}\leq 1+x
for
x<1
,
in the penultimate inequality we used the elementary fact

(1-x)(1+\frac{1}{16}x)^{2}\leq 1-\frac{5}{6}x
for
x\in[0,1]
,
and in the last inequality we used the obvious fact

\|H_{t}\|=\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}((1-\eta)\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\|\leq\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\|=\|Z_{t}\|.

The desired inequality (
95
) follows from the above inequality by setting
C_{\ref{lem:misalign-update}}=800
.

For the remaining claim, we need to apply the conclusion
of the first part with
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}=\|\cdot\|
.
Then we note the following bounds:

(i)

\|Z_{t}\|\leq\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\|^{2}(\|\widetilde{S}_{t}\|^{2}+\lambda)\leq(C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}+1)^{2}\kappa^{4}

by (
22d
) and (
12b
) (since we may choose
c_{\lambda}\leq 1
);

(ii)

\eta\leq c_{\eta}\leq(C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}+1)^{2}\kappa^{4}
;

(iii)

\|U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}\|\leq\|\Delta_{t}\|\leq 16(C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}+1)^{2}c_{\delta}\kappa^{-2C_{\delta}/3}\|X_{\star}\|^{2}

by Lemma
12
;

(iv)

(\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|/\sigma_{\min}(\widetilde{S}_{t}))^{1/2}\leq c_{\delta}\kappa^{-2C_{\delta}/3}

by (
22a
), if we choose
C_{\alpha}\geq 3c_{\delta}^{-1}+3C_{\delta}+3
.

These together imply

\|\widetilde{N}_{t+1}\widetilde{S}_{t+1}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|\leq\left(1-\frac{\eta}{6(C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}+1)^{2}\kappa^{4}}\right)\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|+\eta\frac{16C_{\ref{lem:misalign-update}}\kappa^{2}}{c_{\lambda}}(C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}+1)^{2}c_{\delta}\kappa^{-2C_{\delta}/3}\|X_{\star}\|+\eta c_{\delta}\kappa^{-2C_{\delta}/3}\|X_{\star}\|.

(107)

The conclusion follows easily by plugging in

\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|\leq c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}\kappa^{-C_{\delta}/2}\|X_{\star}\|

and using
\kappa^{6}\kappa^{-2C_{\delta}/3}\leq\kappa^{-C_{\delta}/2}

when
C_{\delta}
is sufficiently large.

Proof of bound (
98
).

First, we observe that it is equivalent to show that
\sigma_{\min}(V_{t}^{\top}V_{t+1})\geq 1/2
.
But from
V_{t+1}V_{t+1}^{\top}+V_{{t+1,\perp}}V_{{t+1,\perp}}^{\top}=I
we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{\min}(V_{t}^{\top}V_{t+1})

\displaystyle=\sigma_{r_{\star}}(V_{t}^{\top}V_{t+1})\geq\sigma_{r_{\star}}(V_{t}^{\top}V_{t+1}V_{t+1}^{\top})=\sigma_{r_{\star}}(V_{t}^{\top}-V_{t}^{\top}V_{{t+1,\perp}}V_{{t+1,\perp}}^{\top})

\displaystyle\geq\sigma_{r_{\star}}(V_{t}^{\top})-\|V_{t}^{\top}V_{{t+1,\perp}}V_{{t+1,\perp}}^{\top}\|

\displaystyle\geq 1-\|V_{t}^{\top}V_{{t+1,\perp}}\|,

where the last inequality follows from
\sigma_{r_{\star}}(V_{t}^{\top})=1

(since
V_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r_{\star}}
is orthonormal)
and from that
\|V_{t}^{\top}V_{{t+1,\perp}}V_{{t+1,\perp}}^{\top}\|\leq\|V_{t}^{\top}V_{{t+1,\perp}}\|
.
This implies that, to show
\sigma_{\min}(V_{t}^{\top}V_{t+1})\geq 1/2
,
it suffices to prove
\|V_{t}^{\top}V_{{t+1,\perp}}\|\leq 1/2
.

Next we prove that
\|V_{t}^{\top}V_{{t+1,\perp}}\|\leq 1/2
.
Recall that by definition we have
S_{t+1}V_{{t+1,\perp}}=0
.
Right-multiplying both sides of (
53a
)
by
V_{{t+1,\perp}}
, we obtain

0=\left((1-\eta)I+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I+{E}^{a}_{t})(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\right)\widetilde{S}_{t}(V_{t}^{\top}V_{{t+1,\perp}})+\eta{E}^{b}_{t}V_{{t+1,\perp}},

hence

\|V_{t}^{\top}V_{{t+1,\perp}}\|\leq\eta\|{E}^{b}_{t}V_{{t+1,\perp}}\|\|\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\|\left\|\left((1-\eta)I+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I+{E}^{a}_{t})(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\right)^{-1}\right\|.

By (
54b
) we have

\|{E}^{b}_{t}V_{{t+1,\perp}}\|\|\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\|\leq\frac{\|{E}^{b}_{t}\|}{\sigma_{\min}(\widetilde{S}_{t})}\leq\frac{1}{10\kappa},

thus it suffices to show

\eta\left\|\left((1-\eta)I+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I+{E}^{a}_{t})(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\right)^{-1}\right\|\leq 5\kappa,

(108)

or equivalently,

\sigma_{\min}\left((1-\eta)I+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I+{E}^{a}_{t})(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\right)\geq\frac{\eta}{5\kappa}.

(109)

To this end, we write

\displaystyle(1-\eta)I+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I+{E}^{a}_{t})(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\left(I+\eta{E}^{a}_{t}\left((1-\eta)(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)\right)^{-1}\right)\left((1-\eta)I+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\right)

(110)

and control the two terms separately.

•

To control the first factor,
starting from (
54a
) we may deduce

\displaystyle\|{E}^{a}_{t}\|

\displaystyle\leq\kappa^{-4}\|X_{\star}\|\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|+\|U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}\|

\displaystyle\leq\kappa^{-4}\|X_{\star}\|c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}\kappa^{-C_{\delta}/2}\|X_{\star}\|+c_{\ref{lem:Delta-bound}}\kappa^{-2C_{\delta}/3}\|X_{\star}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa^{-2}\|X_{\star}\|^{2}/2=\sigma_{\min}^{2}(X_{\star})/2,

where the second inequality follows from (
22c
)
and Lemma
12
;
the last inequality follows from choosing
c_{\delta}
sufficiently small
(recall that
c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}},c_{\ref{lem:Delta-bound}}\lesssim c_{\delta}/c_{\lambda}^{3}
)
and
C_{\delta}
sufficiently large.
Furthermore, since
\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}
is positive semidefinite, we have

\left\|\left((1-\eta)(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)\right)^{-1}\right\|\leq\eta^{-1}\sigma_{\min}^{-2}(\Sigma_{\star})=\eta^{-1}\sigma_{\min}^{-2}(X_{\star}),

hence

\displaystyle\sigma_{\min}\left(1+\eta{E}^{a}_{t}\left((1-\eta)(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)\right)^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\eta\|{E}^{a}_{t}\|\left\|\left((1-\eta)(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)\right)^{-1}\right\|

\displaystyle\geq 1-\eta\cdot\frac{\sigma_{\min}^{2}(X_{\star})}{2}\cdot\eta^{-1}\sigma_{\min}^{-2}(X_{\star})=1/2.

(111)

•

Now we control the second factor.
By Lemma
10
we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{\min}\left(1-\eta+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle=(1-\eta)\sigma_{\min}\left(I+\frac{\eta}{1-\eta}(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle\geq(1-\eta)\left(\frac{\|\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I\|}{\sigma_{\min}(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)}\right)^{-1/2}

\displaystyle=(1-\eta)\left(\frac{\|X_{\star}\|^{2}+\lambda}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}(X_{\star})+\lambda}\right)^{-1/2}.

It is easy to check that the function
\lambda\mapsto(a+\lambda)/(b+\lambda)

is decreasing on
[0,\infty)
for
a\geq b>0
,
thus

\frac{\|X_{\star}\|^{2}+\lambda}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}(X_{\star})+\lambda}\leq\frac{\|X_{\star}\|^{2}}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}(X_{\star})}=\kappa^{2},

which implies

\sigma_{\min}\left((1-\eta)I+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\right)\geq\frac{1-\eta}{\kappa}.

(112)

Plugging (
112
) and (
•
‣
C.2.2
) into (
110
) yields

\sigma_{\min}\left((1-\eta)I+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I+{E}^{a}_{t})(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\right)\geq\frac{1-\eta}{2\kappa}\geq\frac{\eta}{5\kappa},

(113)

where the last inequality follows from the assumption
\eta\leq c_{\eta}
.
This shows (
109
) as desired,
thereby completing the proof.

C.2.3
Proof of Lemma
25

Combine (
100b
) and Lemma
14
to see that

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{S}_{t+1}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|S_{t+1}V_{t}+S_{t+1}V_{{t,\perp}}Q\|

\displaystyle\leq\|1+\eta{E}^{\ref{lem:S-surrogate}}_{t}\|\cdot\left\|(1-\eta)(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{1/2}+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\right\|\cdot\left\|(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\widetilde{S}_{t}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq(1+\eta\|{E}^{\ref{lem:S-surrogate}}_{t}\|)\left((1-\eta)(\|\widetilde{S}_{t}\|^{2}+\lambda)^{1/2}+4\eta\lambda^{-1/2}\|X_{\star}\|^{2}\right)(\|\widetilde{S}_{t}\|^{2}+\lambda)^{-1/2}\|\widetilde{S}_{t}\|

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\frac{\eta}{4}\right)\left((1-\eta)\|\widetilde{S}_{t}\|+4\eta\frac{\|X_{\star}\|^{2}\|\widetilde{S}_{t}\|}{\sqrt{\lambda(\|\widetilde{S}_{t}\|^{2}+\lambda)}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\frac{\eta}{2}\right)\|\widetilde{S}_{t}\|+5\eta\frac{\|X_{\star}\|^{2}}{\sqrt{\lambda}},

(114)

where the third line follows from
\|\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I\|\leq(1+\lambda)\|X_{\star}\|^{2}\leq 2\|X_{\star}\|^{2}
assuming
c_{\lambda}\leq 1

and from the fact that the singular values of
(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\widetilde{S}_{t}
are

(\sigma_{j}^{2}(\widetilde{S}_{t})+\lambda)^{-1/2}\sigma_{j}(\widetilde{S}_{t})
,
j=1,\ldots,r_{\star}
,
4
4

4

This can be seen from plugging in
\widetilde{S}_{t}=U_{t}\Sigma_{t}
by definition which implies
(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\widetilde{S}_{t}=U_{t}(\Sigma_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\Sigma_{t}
.

which is bounded by
(\|\widetilde{S}_{t}\|^{2}+\lambda)^{-1/2}\|\widetilde{S}_{t}\|
since
\sigma\mapsto(\sigma^{2}+\lambda)^{-1/2}\sigma
is increasing and since
\|\widetilde{S}_{t}\|
is the largest singular value of
\widetilde{S}_{t}
. In the fourth line,
we used the error bound
\|{E}^{\ref{lem:S-surrogate}}_{t}\|\leq 1/4

and the last line follows from the elementary inequalities
1+\eta/4\leq(1-\eta/2)(1-\eta)^{-1}\leq 5/4

given that
\eta\leq c_{\eta}
for sufficiently small constant
c_{\eta}>0
.
The conclusion readily follows from the above inequality and the assumption

\lambda\geq\frac{1}{100}c_{\lambda}\sigma_{\min}^{2}(X_{\star})
.

Appendix D
Proofs for Phase II

This section collects the proofs for Phase II.

D.1
Proof of Lemma
4

Since
\|V_{t+1}^{\top}V_{t}\|\leq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{\min}((\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\widetilde{S}_{t+1})

\displaystyle\geq\sigma_{\min}((\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\widetilde{S}_{t+1}V_{t+1}^{\top}V_{t})

\displaystyle=\sigma_{\min}((\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}S_{t+1}V_{t}),

where the second equality follows from
S_{t+1}=\widetilde{S}_{t+1}V_{t+1}^{\top}
(cf. (
31
)).
Apply Lemma
14
with
Q=0
to see that

S_{t+1}V_{t}=(I+\eta{E}^{\ref{lem:S-surrogate}}_{t})\left((1-\eta)I+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\right)\widetilde{S}_{t},

(115)

where
{E}^{\ref{lem:S-surrogate}}_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{\star}\times r_{\star}}
satisfies
\|{E}^{\ref{lem:S-surrogate}}_{t}\|\leq\frac{1}{200(C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}+1)^{4}\kappa^{5}}
.
To simplify the notation, we denote

Y_{t}\coloneqq(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\widetilde{S}_{t},

which allows us to write (
115
) as

\displaystyle(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}S_{t+1}V_{t}

\displaystyle\quad=\left(I+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}{E}^{\ref{lem:S-surrogate}}_{t}(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{1/2}\right)\Big((1-\eta)I+\eta\big(Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}+\lambda(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1}\big)^{-1}\Big)Y_{t}.

(116)

Note that

\displaystyle\|(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}{E}^{\ref{lem:S-surrogate}}_{t}(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{1/2}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\|\cdot\|(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{1/2}\|\cdot\|{E}^{\ref{lem:S-surrogate}}_{t}\|

\displaystyle\leq\kappa\|X_{\star}\|^{-1}\cdot(2\|X_{\star}\|)\cdot\|{E}^{\ref{lem:S-surrogate}}_{t}\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\kappa\cdot\frac{1}{200(C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}+1)^{4}\kappa^{5}}\leq 1/32,

(117)

where in the second inequality we used
\lambda\leq c_{\lambda}\|M_{\star}\|\leq\|X_{\star}\|^{2}

as
c_{\lambda}\leq 1
, and in the third inequality we used the claimed bound of
\|{E}^{\ref{lem:S-surrogate}}_{t}\|
. Therefore, it follows that

\sigma_{\min}\left(I+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}{E}^{\ref{lem:S-surrogate}}_{t}(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{1/2}\right)\geq 1-\eta/32.

(118)

On the other hand, using
\sigma_{\min}(AB)\geq\sigma_{\min}(A)\sigma_{\min}(B)
for any matrices
A,B
, it is obvious that

\sigma_{\min}\Big(\!\left((1-\eta)I+\eta(Y_{t}Y_{t}^{\top}+\lambda(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1})^{-1}\right)Y_{t}\Big)\geq(1-\eta)\sigma_{\min}(Y_{t}),

which in turn implies that

\sigma_{\min}\big((\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}S_{t+1}V_{t}\big)\geq(1-\eta/32)(1-\eta)\sigma_{\min}(Y_{t})\geq(1-2\eta)\sigma_{\min}(Y_{t}),

as long as
\eta\leq c_{\eta}
for some sufficiently small constant
c_{\eta}
.
This proves the first part of Lemma
4
.

Now we move to the second part assuming
\sigma_{\min}(Y_{t})\leq 1/3
.
Using the assumption
\lambda\leq c_{\lambda}\sigma_{\min}(M_{\star})
, we see that

\|\lambda(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1}\|\leq c_{\lambda}.

Given that
c_{\lambda}
is sufficiently small (such that
c_{\lambda}\leq c_{\ref{lem:la-aux}}
, where
c_{\ref{lem:la-aux}}
is the positive constant in Lemma
11
),
one may apply Lemma
11
with
Y=Y_{t}
and

\Lambda=\lambda(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1}
to obtain

\displaystyle\sigma_{\min}\big((\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}S_{t+1}V_{t}\big)

\displaystyle\geq\sigma_{\min}\left(I+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}{E}^{\ref{lem:S-surrogate}}_{t}(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{1/2}\right)\left(1+\frac{1}{6}\eta\right)\sigma_{\min}(Y_{t})

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(i)}}{\geq}(1-\eta/32)\left(1+\frac{1}{6}\eta\right)\sigma_{\min}(Y_{t})\overset{\mathrm{(ii)}}{\geq}\left(1+\frac{1}{8}\eta\right)\sigma_{\min}(Y_{t}),

where (i) uses (
118
), and (ii) follows as long as
\eta\leq c_{\eta}
for some sufficiently small constant
c_{\eta}
. The desired conclusion follows.

D.2
Proof of Corollary
1

We will prove a strengthened version of (
25
), that is

\sigma_{\min}\left((\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\widetilde{S}_{t}\right)\geq 1/\sqrt{10}.

(119)

It is clear that (
119
) implies (
25
). Indeed, for each
u\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{\star}}
, by taking
v=(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{1/2}u
, we have

\displaystyle u^{\top}\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}u=v^{\top}(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}v\geq\frac{1}{10}\|v\|^{2}\geq\frac{1}{10}u^{\top}\Sigma_{\star}^{2}u,

which implies (
25
). It then boils down to establish (
119
).

Step 1: establishing the claim for a midpoint
t_{2}
.

From Lemma
3
we know that

\sigma_{\min}\left((\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\widetilde{S}_{t_{1}}\right)\geq\|\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I\|^{-1/2}\sigma_{\min}(\widetilde{S}_{t_{1}})\overset{\mathrm{(i)}}{\geq}(c_{\lambda}+1)^{-1/2}\|X_{\star}\|^{-1}\cdot\alpha^{2}/\|X_{\star}\|\geq\frac{1}{3}(\alpha/\|X_{\star}\|)^{2},

where (i) follows from
the assumption (
12b
) and Lemma
3
,
and the last inequality follows by choosing
c_{\lambda}\leq 1
.
By the second part of Lemma
4
, starting from
t_{1}
,
whenever
\sigma_{\min}((\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\widetilde{S}_{t})<1/\sqrt{10}<1/3
, it would increase exponentially with rate at least
(1+\frac{\eta}{8})
. On the other end, it is easy to verify,
given that
\eta\leq c_{\eta}
is sufficiently small,

\left(1+\frac{\eta}{8}\right)^{\frac{16}{\eta}\log\big(\frac{3}{\sqrt{10}}\frac{\|X_{\star}\|^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\big)}\geq\frac{3\|X_{\star}\|^{2}}{\sqrt{10}\alpha^{2}}\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{10}}\frac{1}{\sigma_{\min}\left((\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\widetilde{S}_{t_{1}}\right)}.

Therefore, it takes at most
\frac{16}{\eta}\log\Big(\frac{3}{\sqrt{10}}\frac{\|X_{\star}\|^{2}}{\alpha^{2}}\Big)\leq T_{\min}/16
more iterations to make
\sigma_{\min}((\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\widetilde{S}_{t})

grow to at least
1/\sqrt{10}
. Equivalent, for some
t_{2}:t_{1}\leq t_{2}\leq t_{1}+T_{\min}/16
,
we have

\sigma_{\min}\left((\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\widetilde{S}_{t_{2}}\right)\geq 1/\sqrt{10}.

Step 2: establishing the claim for all
t\in[t_{2},T_{\max}]
.

It remains to show that (
119
)
continues to hold for all
t\in[t_{2},T_{\max}]
.
We prove this by induction on
t
.
Assume that (
119
) holds for some
t\in[t_{2},T_{\max}-1]
.
We show that it will also hold for
t+1
.
We divide the proof into two cases.

Case 1.

If
\sigma_{\min}((\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\widetilde{S}_{t})\leq 1/3
,
we deduce from the second part of Lemma
4
that

\sigma_{\min}\left((\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\widetilde{S}_{t+1}\right)\geq\left(1+\frac{\eta}{8}\right)\sigma_{\min}\left((\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\widetilde{S}_{t}\right)\geq\sigma_{\min}\left((\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\widetilde{S}_{t}\right),

which by the induction hypothesis is no less than
1/\sqrt{10}
, as desired.

Case 2.

If
\sigma_{\min}((\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\widetilde{S}_{t})>1/3
,
the first part of Lemma
4
yields

\sigma_{\min}\left((\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\widetilde{S}_{t+1}\right)\geq(1-2\eta)\sigma_{\min}\left((\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\widetilde{S}_{t}\right)\geq(1-2\eta)/3,

which is greater than
1/\sqrt{10}
provided
\eta\leq c_{\eta}\leq 1/100
,
as desired.

Combining the two cases completes the proof.

D.3
Proof of Lemma
5

For simplicity, in this section we denote

\Gamma_{t}\coloneqq\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}-I=\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma_{\star}^{2})\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}.

(120)

It turns out that Lemma
5
follows naturally from the following
technical lemma, whose proof is deferred to the end of this section.

Lemma 26
.

For any
t:t_{2}\leq t\leq T_{\max}
, one has

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Gamma_{t+1}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\leq(1-\eta){|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Gamma_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\eta\frac{C_{\ref{lem:local-unify}}\kappa^{4}}{\|X_{\star}\|^{2}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\frac{1}{16}\eta\|X_{\star}\|^{-1}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\eta\left(\frac{\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|}{\|X_{\star}\|}\right)^{7/12},

(121)

where
C_{\ref{lem:local-unify}}\lesssim c_{\lambda}^{-1/2}
is some positive constant and
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}
can either be the Frobenius norm or the spectral norm.

From Lemma
12
, we know that

\|U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}\|\leq\|\Delta_{t}\|\leq\frac{\|X_{\star}\|^{2}}{300C_{\ref{lem:local-unify}}\kappa^{4}}

as
c_{\delta}
is sufficiently small.
Similarly,
\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|\leq\|X_{\star}\|/100

and
(\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|/\|X_{\star}\|)^{7/12}\leq 1/300
by Lemma
3
.
Applying Lemma
26
with the spectral norm,
we prove Lemma
5
as desired.

Proof of Lemma
26
.

We start by rewriting (
53a
) as

\displaystyle S_{t+1}

\displaystyle=\big((1-\eta)I+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\big)\widetilde{S}_{t}V_{t}^{\top}+\eta{E}^{g}_{t}

\displaystyle=\big(I-\eta(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}+\eta(\Sigma_{\star}^{2}+\lambda I)(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\big)\widetilde{S}_{t}V_{t}^{\top}+\eta{E}^{g}_{t}

\displaystyle=\big(I-\eta(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma_{\star}^{2})(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\big)\widetilde{S}_{t}V_{t}^{\top}+\eta{E}^{g}_{t},

(122)

where

\displaystyle{E}^{g}_{t}={E}^{a}_{t}(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}V_{t}^{\top}+{E}^{b}_{t}.

(123)

By Corollary
1
, we have
\sigma_{\min}(\widetilde{S}_{t})^{2}\geq\frac{1}{100}\sigma_{\min}(M_{\star})
for
t\in[t_{2},T_{\max}]
,
so

\displaystyle\|(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}V_{t}^{\top}\|\leq\|(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\|\|(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1/2}\widetilde{S}_{t}\|\leq\sigma_{\min}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t})\lesssim 1/\sigma_{\min}(X_{\star}).

Combined with the error bounds (
54a
), (
54b
), we have
for some universal constant
C>0
that

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{E}^{g}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\leq{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{E}^{a}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\eta{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{E}^{b}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\leq\frac{C\kappa}{\|X_{\star}\|}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+Cc_{\ref{lem:update-approx}}\kappa^{-3}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+C\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|^{3/4}\|X_{\star}\|^{1/4}.

(124)

Step 1: deriving a recursion of
\Gamma_{t}
.

Define

A_{t}:=\big(I-\eta(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma_{\star}^{2})(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)^{-1}\big)\widetilde{S}_{t}V_{t}^{\top}.

Then we can rewrite (
122
) as
A_{t}=S_{t+1}-\eta{E}^{g}_{t}
,
and by rearranging
A_{t}A_{t}^{\top}=(S_{t+1}-\eta{E}^{g}_{t})(S_{t+1}-\eta{E}^{g}_{t})^{\top}
in view of (
31
), it follows that

\displaystyle\widetilde{S}_{t+1}\widetilde{S}_{t+1}^{\top}=S_{t+1}S_{t+1}^{\top}

\displaystyle=A_{t}A_{t}^{\top}+\eta(\|S_{t+1}\|+\|{E}^{g}_{t}\|)({E}^{g}_{t}Q_{1}+Q_{2}{{E}^{g}_{t}}^{\top})

\displaystyle=:A_{t}A_{t}^{\top}+\eta{E}^{f}_{t}

for some matrices
Q_{1},Q_{2}
with
\|Q_{1}\|,\|Q_{2}\|\leq 1
.
By mapping both sides of the above equation by
(\cdot)\mapsto\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}(\cdot)\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}-I
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\Gamma_{t+1}

\displaystyle=\big(I-\eta\Gamma_{t}(I+\Gamma_{t}+\lambda\Sigma_{\star}^{-2})^{-1}\big)(\Gamma_{t}+I)\big(I-\eta(I+\Gamma_{t}+\lambda\Sigma_{\star}^{-2})^{-1}\Gamma_{t}\big)-I+\eta\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}{E}^{f}_{t}\Sigma_{\star}^{-1},

(125)

where we recall the definition of
\Gamma_{t}
in (
120
).

Step 2: simplify the recursion.

Note that
\sigma_{\min}(\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t})\geq 1/10
implies
I+\Gamma_{t}\succeq\frac{1}{100}I
.
From our assumption
\lambda\leq c_{\lambda}\sigma_{\min}(M_{\star})
, it follows that

\|\lambda\Sigma_{\star}^{-2}\|\leq c_{\lambda}\leq 1/200\leq\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{\min}(I+\Gamma_{t})
,
thus in virtue of Lemma
9
we have

(I+\Gamma_{t}+\lambda\Sigma_{\star}^{-2})^{-1}=(I+\Gamma_{t})^{-1}+(I+\Gamma_{t})^{-1}(c_{\lambda}Q^{\prime})(I+\Gamma_{t})^{-1},

for some matrix
Q^{\prime}
with
\|Q^{\prime}\|\leq 2
.
Plugging this into (
125
) yields

\displaystyle\Gamma_{t+1}

\displaystyle=\big(I-\eta\Gamma_{t}(I+\Gamma_{t})^{-1}\big)(\Gamma_{t}+I)\big(I-\eta(I+\Gamma_{t})^{-1}\Gamma_{t}\big)+\eta{E}^{h}_{t}+\eta\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}{E}^{f}_{t}\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}

\displaystyle=(1-2\eta)\Gamma_{t}+\eta^{2}\Gamma_{t}^{2}(1+\Gamma_{t})^{-1}+\eta{E}^{h}_{t}+\eta\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}{E}^{f}_{t}\Sigma_{\star}^{-1},

(126)

where the additional error term
{E}^{h}_{t}
is defined by

\displaystyle{E}^{h}_{t}:=

\displaystyle\Gamma_{t}(I+\Gamma_{t})^{-1}(c_{\lambda}Q^{\prime})(1-\eta\Gamma_{t}(I+\Gamma_{t})^{-1})+(1-\eta\Gamma_{t}(I+\Gamma_{t})^{-1})(c_{\lambda}Q^{\prime})(I+\Gamma_{t})^{-1}\Gamma_{t}

\displaystyle+\eta\Gamma_{t}(I+\Gamma_{t})^{-1}(c_{\lambda}Q^{\prime})(I+\Gamma_{t})^{-2}(c_{\lambda}Q^{\prime})(I+\Gamma_{t})^{-1}\Gamma_{t}.

(127)

Step 3: controlling the error terms.

We now control the error terms in (
126
) separately.

•

By (
22d
) we have
\|S_{t+1}\|\leq C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}\kappa\|X_{\star}\|
,
and by controlling the right hand side of (
124
) using (
22c
), (
24
),
and (
50
) in Lemma
12
,
it is evident that
\|{E}^{g}_{t}\|\leq\kappa\|X_{\star}\|
. Hence,
the term
{E}^{f}_{t}
obeys

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{E}^{f}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\leq(C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}+1)\kappa\|X_{\star}\|\cdot{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{E}^{g}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}\left(\kappa^{2}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+c_{\ref{lem:update-approx}}\kappa^{-2}\|X_{\star}\|{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\kappa\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|^{3/4}\|X_{\star}\|^{5/4}\right),

(128)

where
C^{\prime}>0
is again some universal constant.

•

Since
\Gamma_{t}\succeq\frac{1}{100}I-I=-\frac{99}{100}I
as already proved,
it is easy to see that
\|(1+\Gamma_{t})^{-1}\|\leq C

and
\|\Gamma_{t}(1+\Gamma_{t})^{-1}\|\leq C
for some universal constant
C>0
. Thus,

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{E}^{h}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\leq 2c_{\lambda}C(1+\eta C)\|Q^{\prime}\|\cdot{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Gamma_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\eta c_{\lambda}^{2}C^{4}\|Q^{\prime}\|^{2}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Gamma_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\leq\frac{1}{2}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Gamma_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|},

(129)

where the last line follows by using
\|Q^{\prime}\|\leq 2
and by choosing
c_{\lambda}
,
c_{\eta}
sufficiently small.

•

We still need to control
\eta^{2}\Gamma_{t}^{2}(1+\Gamma_{t})^{-1}
. This can be accomplished by invoking
\|\Gamma_{t}(1+\Gamma_{t})^{-1}\|\leq C
again. In fact, we have

\eta^{2}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Gamma_{t}^{2}(1+\Gamma_{t})^{-1}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\leq\eta\cdot\eta\|\Gamma_{t}(1+\Gamma_{t})^{-1}\|\cdot{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Gamma_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\leq\eta\cdot\eta C{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Gamma_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\leq\frac{\eta}{2}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Gamma_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

(130)

provided that
\eta\leq c_{\eta}
is sufficiently small.

Plugging (
128
), (
129
), (
130
) into (
126
),
we readily obtain

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Gamma_{t+1}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\leq(1-2\eta){|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Gamma_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\frac{\eta}{2}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Gamma_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\frac{\eta}{2}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Gamma_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\eta\kappa^{2}\|X_{\star}\|^{-2}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{E}^{f}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\eta){|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Gamma_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\eta\frac{C^{\prime}C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}\kappa^{4}}{\|X_{\star}\|^{2}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\eta c_{\ref{lem:update-approx}}C^{\prime}C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}\|X_{\star}\|^{-1}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\eta C^{\prime}C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}\kappa^{3}\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|^{3/4}\|X_{\star}\|^{-3/4}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\eta){|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Gamma_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\eta\frac{C_{\ref{lem:local-unify}}\kappa^{4}}{\|X_{\star}\|^{2}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\frac{1}{16}\eta\|X_{\star}\|^{-1}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\eta\left(\frac{\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|}{\|X_{\star}\|}\right)^{7/12},

where in the last line we set
C_{\ref{lem:local-unify}}=C^{\prime}C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}
, chose
c_{\ref{lem:update-approx}}
sufficiently small and used (
24
).
Finally note that
C_{\ref{lem:local-unify}}\lesssim C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}\lesssim c_{\lambda}^{-1/2}
as desired.

D.4
Proof of Corollary
2

From Lemma
5
, it is elementary (e.g., by induction on
t
) to show that

\big\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma_{\star}^{2})\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\big\|\leq(1-\eta)^{t-t_{2}}\big\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t_{2}}\widetilde{S}_{t_{2}}^{\top}-\Sigma_{\star}^{2})\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\big\|+\frac{1}{100},\quad\forall t\in[t_{2},T_{\max}].

(131)

Suppose for the moment that

\big\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t_{2}}\widetilde{S}_{t_{2}}^{\top}-\Sigma_{\star}^{2})\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\big\|\leq C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}^{2}\kappa^{4},

(132)

where
C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}
is given in Lemma
3
.
Then given that
\eta\leq c_{\eta}
for some sufficiently small
c_{\eta}
, we have

\log(1-\eta)\geq-\eta/2
. As a result,
if
t_{3}-t_{2}\geq 8\log(10C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}\kappa)/\eta\geq\log(C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}^{-2}\kappa^{-4}/100)/\log(1-\eta)
,
we have
(1-\eta)^{t_{3}-t_{2}}\leq C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}^{-2}\kappa^{-4}/100
.
When
C_{\min}
is sufficiently large we may choose such
t_{3}
which simultaneously satisfies

t_{3}\leq t_{2}+T_{\min}/16\leq T_{\max}
since

8\log(10C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}\kappa)/\eta\leq\frac{C_{\min}}{32\eta}\log(\|X_{\star}\|/\alpha)=T_{\min}/32
.
Invoking (
131
), we obtain

\big\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t_{3}}\widetilde{S}_{t_{3}}^{\top}-\Sigma_{\star}^{2})\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\big\|\leq(C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}^{-2}\kappa^{-4}/100)(C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}^{2}\kappa^{4})+\frac{1}{100}=\frac{1}{50}\leq\frac{1}{10},

(133)

which implies the desired bound (
27
).

Proof of inequality (
132
).

It is straightforward to verify that

\big\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t_{2}}\widetilde{S}_{t_{2}}^{\top}-\Sigma_{\star}^{2})\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\big\|\leq\max\left(\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t_{2}}\|^{2}-1,1-\sigma_{\min}^{2}(\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t_{2}})\right),

which combined with (
22d
) implies that

\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t_{2}}\|^{2}-1\leq\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\|^{2}\|\widetilde{S}_{t_{2}}\|^{2}\leq\sigma_{\min}^{-2}(X_{\star})C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}^{2}\kappa^{2}\|X_{\star}\|^{2}=C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}^{2}\kappa^{4}.

In addition, by Corollary
1
we have

1-\sigma_{\min}^{2}(\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t_{2}})\leq 1-\frac{1}{10}=\frac{9}{10}.

Choosing
C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}
sufficiently large (say
C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}\geq 1
) yields
C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}^{2}\kappa^{4}\geq 9/10
, and hence
the claim (
132
).

Appendix E
Proofs for Phase III

To characterize the behavior of
\|X_{t}X_{t}^{\top}-M_{\star}\|_{\fro}
,
it is particularly helpful to consider the following decomposition
into three error terms related to the signal term, the misalignment term,
and the overparametrization term.

Lemma 27
.

For all
t\geq t_{3}
, as long as

\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma_{\star}^{2})\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\|\leq 1/10
,
one has

\|X_{t}X_{t}^{\top}-M_{\star}\|_{\fro}\leq 4\|X_{\star}\|^{2}\left(\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma_{\star}^{2})\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\|_{\fro}+\|X_{\star}\|^{-1}\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|_{\fro}\right)+4\|X_{\star}\|\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|.

Note that the overparametrization error
\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|
stays small,
as stated in (
22b
) and (
24
).
Therefore we only need to focus on the shrinkage of the first two terms

\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma_{\star}^{2})\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\|_{\fro}+\|X_{\star}\|^{-1}\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|_{\fro}
, which is the focus of the lemma below.

Lemma 28
.

For any
t:t_{3}\leq t\leq T_{\max}
, one has

\displaystyle\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t+1}\widetilde{S}_{t+1}^{\top}-\Sigma_{\star}^{2})\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\|_{\fro}+\|X_{\star}\|^{-1}\|\widetilde{N}_{t+1}\widetilde{S}_{t+1}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|_{\fro}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left(1-\frac{\eta}{10}\right)\left(\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma_{\star}^{2})\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\|_{\fro}+\|X_{\star}\|^{-1}\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|_{\fro}\right)+\eta\left(\frac{\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|}{\|X_{\star}\|}\right)^{1/2}.

(134)

In particular,
\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t+1}\widetilde{S}_{t+1}^{\top}-\Sigma_{\star}^{2})\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\|\leq 1/10

for all
t
such that
t_{3}\leq t\leq T_{\max}
.

We now show how Lemma
6
is implied by the above two lemmas.
To begin with, we apply Lemma
28
repeatedly to obtain the following bound
for all
t\in[t_{3},T_{\max}]
:

\displaystyle\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma_{\star}^{2})\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\|_{\fro}+\|X_{\star}\|^{-1}\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|_{\fro}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left(1-\frac{\eta}{10}\right)^{t-t_{3}}\left(\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t_{3}}\widetilde{S}_{t_{3}}^{\top}-\Sigma_{\star}^{2})\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\|_{\fro}+\|X_{\star}\|^{-1}\|\widetilde{N}_{t_{3}}\widetilde{S}_{t_{3}}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|_{\fro}\right)+10\max_{t_{3}\leq\tau\leq t}\left(\frac{\|\widetilde{O}_{\tau}\|}{\|X_{\star}\|}\right)^{1/2},

(135)

which motivates us to control the error at time
t_{3}
.

We know from Corollary
2
that

\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t_{3}}\widetilde{S}_{t_{3}}^{\top}-\Sigma_{\star}^{2})\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\|\leq 1/10
.
Since
\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t_{3}}\widetilde{S}_{t_{3}}^{\top}-\Sigma_{\star}^{2})\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}

is a
r_{\star}\times r_{\star}
matrix, we have

\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t_{3}}\widetilde{S}_{t_{3}}^{\top}-\Sigma_{\star}^{2})\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\|_{\fro}\leq\sqrt{r_{\star}}/10
.
In addition, we infer from (
22c
) that

\|\widetilde{N}_{t_{3}}\widetilde{S}_{t_{3}}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|_{\fro}\leq\sqrt{r_{\star}}\|\widetilde{N}_{t_{3}}\widetilde{S}_{t_{3}}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|\leq\sqrt{r_{\star}}c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}\kappa^{-C_{\delta}/2}\|X_{\star}\|\leq\sqrt{r_{\star}}\|X_{\star}\|/10,

as long as
c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}
is sufficiently small.
Combine the above two bounds to arrive at the conclusion that

\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t_{3}}\widetilde{S}_{t_{3}}^{\top}-\Sigma_{\star}^{2})\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\|_{\fro}+\|X_{\star}\|^{-1}\|\widetilde{N}_{t_{3}}\widetilde{S}_{t_{3}}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|_{\fro}\leq\frac{\sqrt{r_{\star}}}{10}+\|X_{\star}\|^{-1}\frac{\sqrt{r_{\star}}\|X_{\star}\|}{10}=\frac{\sqrt{r_{\star}}}{5}.

(136)

Combining the two inequalities (
135
) and (
136
) yields
for all
t\in[t_{3},T_{\max}]

\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma_{\star}^{2})\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\|_{\fro}+\|X_{\star}\|^{-1}\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|_{\fro}\leq\frac{1}{5}\left(1-\frac{\eta}{10}\right)^{t-t_{3}}\sqrt{r_{\star}}+10\max_{t_{3}\leq\tau\leq t}\left(\frac{\|\widetilde{O}_{\tau}\|}{\|X_{\star}\|}\right)^{1/2}.

We can then invoke Lemma
27
to see that

\displaystyle\|X_{t}X_{t}^{\top}-M_{\star}\|_{\fro}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4\|X_{\star}\|^{2}}{5}\left(1-\frac{\eta}{10}\right)^{t-t_{3}}\sqrt{r_{\star}}+40\|X_{\star}\|^{2}\max_{t_{3}\leq\tau\leq t}\left(\frac{\|\widetilde{O}_{\tau}\|}{\|X_{\star}\|}\right)^{1/2}+4\|X_{\star}\|\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\frac{\eta}{10}\right)^{t-t_{3}}\sqrt{r_{\star}}\|M_{\star}\|+80\|M_{\star}\|\max_{t_{3}\leq\tau\leq t}\left(\frac{\|\widetilde{O}_{\tau}\|}{\|X_{\star}\|}\right)^{1/2},

where in the last line we use
\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|\leq\|X_{\star}\|
—an implication of (
24
).
To see this, the assumption (
12c
) implies that

\alpha\leq\|X_{\star}\|
as long as
\eta\leq 1/2
and
C_{\alpha}\geq 4
,
which in turn implies
\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|\leq\alpha^{2/3}\|X_{\star}\|^{1/3}\leq\|X_{\star}\|
.
This completes the proof for the first part of Lemma
6
with
c_{\ref{lem:p3}}=1/10
.

For the second part of Lemma
6
,
notice that

8c_{\ref{lem:p3}}^{-1}\max_{t_{3}\leq\tau\leq T_{\max}}(\|\widetilde{O}_{\tau}\|/\|X_{\star}\|)^{1/2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\alpha}{\|X_{\star}\|}\right)^{1/3}

by (
24
), thus

\|X_{t}X_{t}^{\top}-M_{\star}\|_{\fro}\leq(1-c_{\ref{lem:p3}}\eta)^{t-t_{3}}\sqrt{r_{\star}}\|M_{\star}\|+\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\alpha}{\|X_{\star}\|}\right)^{1/3}

for
t_{3}\leq t\leq T_{\max}
.
There exists some iteration number

t_{4}:t_{3}\leq t_{4}\leq t_{3}+\frac{2}{c_{\ref{lem:p3}}\eta}\log(\|X_{\star}\|/\alpha)\leq t_{3}+T_{\min}/16

such that

(1-c_{\ref{lem:p3}}\eta)^{t_{4}-t_{3}}\leq\left(\frac{\alpha}{\|X_{\star}\|}\right)^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{r_{\star}}}\left(\frac{\alpha}{\|X_{\star}\|}\right)^{1/3},

where the last inequality is due to (
12c
).
It is then clear that
t_{4}
has the property claimed in the lemma.

E.1
Proof of Lemma
27

Starting from (
51
),
we may deduce

\displaystyle\|X_{t}X_{t}^{\top}-M_{\star}\|_{\fro}

\displaystyle\leq\|\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma_{\star}^{2}\|_{\fro}+2\|\widetilde{S}_{t}\|\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\|_{\fro}+\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\|\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\|_{\fro}+\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|_{\fro}

\displaystyle\leq\|X_{\star}\|^{2}\left(\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}-I\|_{\fro}+2\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}\|^{2}\|X_{\star}\|^{-1}\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|_{\fro}+\sqrt{n}\left(\frac{\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|}{\|X_{\star}\|}\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 4\|X_{\star}\|^{2}\left(\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}-I\|_{\fro}+\|X_{\star}\|^{-1}\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|_{\fro}+\frac{\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|}{\|X_{\star}\|}\right),

(137)

where the penultimate line used
\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|_{\fro}\leq\sqrt{n}\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|
,
and the last line follows from
\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}\|^{2}=\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\|\leq 1+\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}-I\|\leq 2

(recall that
\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}-I\|\leq 1/10

by assumption)
and from (
24
).

E.2
Proof of Lemma
28

Recall the definition of
\Gamma_{t}
from (
120
):

\displaystyle\Gamma_{t}\coloneqq\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}-I.

Fix any
t\in[t_{3},T_{\max}]
, if (
134
) were true for all
\tau\in[t_{3},t]
,
taking into account that
\|\widetilde{O}_{\tau}\|/\|X_{\star}\|\leq 1/10000

for all
\tau\in[t_{3},T_{\max}]
by (
24
),
we could show by induction that
\|\Gamma_{\tau}\|\leq 1/10
for all
\tau\in[t_{3},t]
.
Thus it suffices to assume
\|\Gamma_{t}\|\leq 1/10
and prove (
134
).

Apply Lemma
26
with Frobenius norm to obtain

\|{\Gamma_{t+1}}\|_{\fro}\leq(1-\eta)\|{\Gamma_{t}}\|_{\fro}+\eta\frac{C_{\ref{lem:local-unify}}\kappa^{4}}{\|X_{\star}\|^{2}}\|{U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}}\|_{\fro}+\frac{1}{16}\eta\|X_{\star}\|^{-1}\|{\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}}\|_{\fro}+\eta\left(\frac{\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|}{\|X_{\star}\|}\right)^{7/12},

(138)

In addition, Lemma
24
tells us that

\|{\widetilde{N}_{t+1}\widetilde{S}_{t+1}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}}\|_{\fro}\leq\left(1-\frac{\eta}{3(\|Z_{t}\|+\eta)}\right)\|{\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}}\|_{\fro}+\eta\frac{C_{\ref{lem:misalign-update}}\kappa^{2}}{c_{\lambda}\|X_{\star}\|}\|{U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}}\|_{\fro}+\eta\left(\frac{\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|}{\sigma_{\min}(\widetilde{S}_{t})}\right)^{2/3}\|X_{\star}\|,

where
Z_{t}=\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}
.
It is easy to check that
\|Z_{t}\|\leq 1+\|\Gamma_{t}\|+c_{\lambda}\leq 2
as
\|\Gamma_{t}\|\leq 1/10
and
c_{\lambda}
is sufficiently small.
In addition, one has
\sigma_{\min}(\widetilde{S}_{t})^{2}\geq(1-\|\Gamma_{t}\|)\sigma_{\min}(X_{\star})^{2}

and
\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|/\sigma_{\min}(\widetilde{S}_{t})\leq(2\kappa)^{-24}
.
Combine these relationships together to arrive at

\|{\widetilde{N}_{t+1}\widetilde{S}_{t+1}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}}\|_{\fro}\leq\left(1-\frac{\eta}{8}\right)\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|_{\fro}+\eta\frac{C_{\ref{lem:misalign-update}}\kappa^{2}}{c_{\lambda}\|X_{\star}\|}\|U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}\|_{\fro}+\frac{1}{2}\eta\|X_{\star}\|\left(\frac{\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|}{\|X_{\star}\|}\right)^{7/12}.

(139)

Summing up (
138
), (
139
),
we obtain

\displaystyle\|\Gamma_{t+1}\|_{\fro}+\|X_{\star}\|^{-1}\|\widetilde{N}_{t+1}\widetilde{S}_{t+1}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|_{\fro}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left(1-\frac{\eta}{8}\right)(\|\Gamma_{t}\|_{\fro}+\|X_{\star}\|^{-1}\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|_{\fro})+\eta\frac{2(C_{\ref{lem:misalign-update}}+C_{\ref{lem:local-unify}}c_{\lambda})\kappa^{4}}{c_{\lambda}\|X_{\star}\|^{2}}\|U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}\|_{\fro}+2\eta\left(\frac{\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|}{\|X_{\star}\|}\right)^{7/12}.

(140)

This is close to our desired conclusion,
but we would need to eliminate
\|U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}\|_{\fro}
.
To this end we observe

\displaystyle\|U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}\|_{\fro}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{r_{\star}}\|\Delta_{t}\|

\displaystyle\leq 8\delta\sqrt{r_{\star}}\left(\|\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma_{\star}^{2}\|_{\fro}+\|\widetilde{S}_{t}\|\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\|_{\fro}+n\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 16c_{\delta}\kappa^{-4}\|X_{\star}\|^{2}\left(\|\Gamma_{t}\|_{\fro}+\|X_{\star}\|^{-1}\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|_{\fro}+\left(\frac{\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|}{\|X_{\star}\|}\right)^{2/3}\right),

where the first line follows from
U_{\star}
being of rank
r_{\star}
,
the second line follows from Lemma
12
,
and the last line follows from (
10
)
and from controlling the sum inside the brackets
in a similar way as (
137
).

The conclusion follows from plugging
the above inequality into (
140
),
noting that
c_{\delta}
can be chosen sufficiently small
and that
\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|/\|X_{\star}\|

is sufficiently small due to (
24
).

Appendix F
Proofs for the noisy and the approximate low-rank settings

Both Theorem
4
and Theorem
5
can be viewed as special cases of the following theorem.

Theorem 6
.

Assume the iterates
X_{t}
of
ScaledGD(
\lambda
)
obeys

X_{t+1}=X_{t}-\eta(\mathcal{A^{*}A}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\top}-M_{\star})-E)X_{t}(X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1},

(141)

for some matrix
E\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
, where
M_{\star}=X_{\star}X_{\star}^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
is a positive semidefinite matrix of rank
r_{\star}
,
X_{\star}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times r_{\star}}
.
Assume further that

\|E\|\leq c_{\sigma}\kappa^{-C_{\sigma}}\|M_{\star}\|

(142)

for some sufficiently small universal constant
c_{\sigma}>0
and some sufficiently large universal constant
C_{\sigma}>0
. Then the following holds with high probability (with respect to the realization of the random initialization
G
). Under Assumptions
1
and
2
, there exist universal constants
C_{\min}>0
,
C_{\ref{lem:unified-noisy}}>0
, such that for some
T\leq T_{\min}\coloneqq\frac{C_{\min}}{\eta}\log\frac{\|X_{\star}\|}{\alpha}
, the iterates of (
141
) obey

\displaystyle\|X_{T}X_{T}^{\top}-M_{\star}\|

\displaystyle\leq\max\left(\varepsilon\|M_{\star}\|,~C_{\ref{lem:unified-noisy}}\kappa^{4}\|U_{\star}^{\top}E\|\right),

\displaystyle\|X_{T}X_{T}^{\top}-M_{\star}\|_{\fro}

\displaystyle\leq\max\left(\varepsilon\|M_{\star}\|,~C_{\ref{lem:unified-noisy}}\kappa^{4}\|U_{\star}^{\top}E\|_{\fro}\right).

The proof is postponed to Appendix
G
. The rest of this appendix is devoted to showing how to deduce Theorem
4
and Theorem
5
from Theorem
6
.

F.1
Proof of Theorem
4

In the noisy setting, the update rule (
14
) of
ScaledGD(
\lambda
)
can be written as

X_{t+1}=X_{t}-\eta\big(\mathcal{A^{*}A}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\top}-M_{\star})-E\big)X_{t}(X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1},

(143)

where

E\coloneqq\mathcal{A}^{*}(\xi)=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\xi_{i}A_{i}.

(144)

We use the following classical lemma to show that the matrix
E
defined above fulfills the assumption of Theorem
6
.

Lemma 29
.

Under Assumption
1
, the following holds with probability at least
1-2\exp(-cn)
.

\displaystyle\|E\|\leq 8\sigma\sqrt{n},\quad\|U_{\star}^{\top}E\|_{\fro}\leq 8\sigma\sqrt{nr_{\star}}.

Proof.

The first inequality can be found in
Candès and Plan, 2010
, Lemma 1.1. The second inequality can be deduced from the first one as follows. Note that
U_{\star}^{\top}E
has rank at most
r_{\star}
, one has
\|U_{\star}^{\top}E\|_{\fro}\leq\sqrt{r_{\star}}\|U_{\star}^{\top}E\|\leq\sqrt{r_{\star}}\|E\|\leq 8\sigma\sqrt{nr_{\star}}
, as desired.
∎

The conclusion of Theorem
4
follows immediately by conditioning on the event that the inequalities in Lemma
29
hold, and then invoking Theorem
6
.

F.2
Proof of Theorem
5

In the approximately low-rank setting, the update rule of
ScaledGD(
\lambda
)
can be written as

X_{t+1}=X_{t}-\eta\big(\mathcal{A^{*}A}(X_{t}X_{t}^{\top}-M_{r_{\star}})-E\big)X_{t}(X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1},

(145)

where

E\coloneqq\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A}(M_{r_{\star}}^{\prime}).

(146)

Recall that we assumed
\mathcal{A}
follows the Gaussian design in Theorem
5
. One may show that the matrix
E
defined above fulfills the assumption of Theorem
6
using random matrix theory, detailed below.

Lemma 30
.

Under the assumptions on
\mathcal{A}
and
m
in Theorem
5
, the following holds with probability at least
1-2\exp(-cn)
.

\displaystyle\|E\|\leq 2\|M_{r_{\star}}^{\prime}\|+16\sqrt{\frac{n}{m}}\|M_{r_{\star}}^{\prime}\|_{\fro},\quad\|U_{\star}^{\top}E\|_{\fro}\leq 16\|M_{r_{\star}}^{\prime}\|_{\fro}.

Proof.

For the first inequality, we use a standard covering argument. Let
\mathcal{H}
be a
1/4
-net of
\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
, which can be chosen to satisfy
|\mathcal{H}|\leq 9^{n}
. It is well known that

\|\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{r_{\star}}^{\prime})\|=\sup_{v\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}|\langle v,\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{r_{\star}}^{\prime})v\rangle|\leq 2\sup_{v\in\mathcal{H}}|\langle v,\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{r_{\star}}^{\prime})v\rangle|.

(147)

Note that
\langle v,\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{r_{\star}}^{\prime})v\rangle
is an order-
2
Gaussian chaos, which can be bounded by standard methods (see e.g.
Candès and Plan, 2010
), yielding

|\langle v,\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{r_{\star}}^{\prime})v\rangle-\langle v,M_{r_{\star}}^{\prime}v\rangle|=|\langle v,\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{r_{\star}}^{\prime})v\rangle-\mathbb{E}\langle v,\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{r_{\star}}^{\prime})v\rangle|\leq 8\sqrt{\frac{n}{m}}\|M_{r_{\star}}^{\prime}\|_{\fro}

with probability at least
1-2\exp(-4n)
. The desired inequality then follows from (
147
) and a union bound.

For the second inequality, we first note that the random vector
\mathcal{A}(M_{r_{\star}}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
is Gaussian with law
\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{1}{m}\|M_{r_{\star}}^{\prime}\|_{\fro}^{2}I)
. A standard Gaussian concentration inequality implies
\|\mathcal{A}(M_{r_{\star}}^{\prime})\|\leq 2\|M_{r_{\star}}^{\prime}\|_{\fro}
with probability at least
1-2\exp(-m/2)
. To bound
\|U_{\star}^{\top}\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{r_{\star}}^{\prime})\|_{\fro}
, the next step is to control the operator norm of
U_{\star}^{\top}\mathcal{A}^{*}
as an operator on the following spaces:

U_{\star}^{\top}\mathcal{A}^{*}:\quad(\mathbb{R}^{m},\ell_{2})\to\underbrace{(\mathbb{R}^{r_{\star}\times n},\|\cdot\|_{\fro})}_{\eqqcolon\mathcal{M}}.

In this sense, we may see that
U_{\star}^{\top}\mathcal{A}^{*}
is a Gaussian operator, since the matrix form of this operator is a
(r_{\star}n)\times m
matrix whose
i
-th column is the vectorization of
U_{\star}^{\top}A_{i}
, which is i.i.d. Gaussian as
A_{i}
is.
Assume the covariance of such a column is
\Lambda^{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{(r_{\star}n)\times(r_{\star}n)}
, then the matrix form of
U_{\star}^{\top}\mathcal{A}^{*}
has the same distribution as
\Lambda G
, where
G
is a
(r_{\star}n)\times m
random matrix with i.i.d. standard Gaussian entries. Again, a standard bound in random matrix theory (c.f. (
30a
)) implies that
\|G\|\leq 4(\sqrt{m}+\sqrt{r_{\star}n})
with probability at least
1-\exp(cm)
, given
m\geq Cnr_{\star}
as assumed in Theorem
5
. Conditioning on this event, we have

\|U_{\star}^{\top}\mathcal{A}^{*}\|\leq 4(\sqrt{m}+\sqrt{r_{\star}n})\|\Lambda\|.

To compute
\|\Lambda\|
, note that since
\Lambda G
has the same distribution as the matrix form of
U_{\star}^{\top}\mathcal{A}^{*}
, we have

\|\mathbb{E}(U_{\star}^{\top}\mathcal{A^{*}A}U_{\star})\|_{\mathcal{M}}=\|\mathbb{E}(\Lambda GG^{\top}\Lambda)\|=\|\Lambda(mI)\Lambda\|=m\|\Lambda\|^{2},

where the norm
\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{M}}
denotes the operator norm for operators on
\mathcal{M}
.
But
\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{A^{*}A})=\mathcal{I}
, thus
\mathbb{E}(U_{\star}^{\top}\mathcal{A^{*}A}U_{\star})=U_{\star}^{\top}U_{\star}=I
is the identity operator, hence
\|\mathbb{E}(U_{\star}^{\top}\mathcal{A^{*}A}U_{\star})\|_{\mathcal{M}}=1
. Plugging this into the above identity, we find
\|\Lambda\|=1/\sqrt{m}
.
These together imply

\|U_{\star}^{\top}\mathcal{A}^{*}\|\leq 4\left(\sqrt{m}+\sqrt{r_{\star}n}\right)\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}=4\left(1+\sqrt{\frac{r_{\star}n}{m}}\right)

with probability at least
1-2\exp(-cm)
. The last quantity is less than
8
by the assumption
m\geq Cnr_{\star}
in Theorem
5
. Therefore

\|U_{\star}^{\top}\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{r_{\star}}^{\prime})\|_{\fro}\leq\|U_{\star}^{\top}\mathcal{A}^{*}\|\cdot\|\mathcal{A}(M_{r_{\star}}^{\prime})\|\leq 8\cdot 2\|M_{r_{\star}}^{\prime}\|_{\fro}=16\|M_{r_{\star}}^{\prime}\|_{\fro}

with probability at least
1-\exp(-cm)
, as desired.
∎

The conclusion of Theorem
5
follows immediately by conditioning on the event that the inequalities in Lemma
30
hold, and then invoking Theorem
6
with
M_{\star}
substituted by
M_{r_{\star}}
.

Appendix G
Proof of Theorem
6

The proof is based on a reduction to the noiseless setting.
We begin with two heuristic observations that connect the generalized setting with the noiseless one, and make these observations formal later.

Observation 1: Phase I approximates power method for
\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})+E
.

As in the noiseless setting, in the first few iterations we expect
\|X_{t}\|
to remain small, thus the update equation (
14
) can be approximated by

X_{t+1}\approx(I+\eta(\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})+E))X_{t}.

This coincides with the update equation of power method for
\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})+E
. Recall that in the noiseless setting, the first phase is also akin to power method, albeit for
\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})
. The key observation is that
\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})+E
enjoys all the same properties of
\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})
that were required to establish Lemma
19
. In fact, the only property of
\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})
used in the proof of Lemma
19
is

\|(\mathcal{A^{*}A}-\mathcal{I})M_{\star}\|\lesssim c_{\delta}\kappa^{-2C_{\delta}/3},

but by the assumption (
142
),
\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})+E
also satisfies

\|\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})+E-M_{\star}\|\leq\|(\mathcal{A^{*}A}-\mathcal{I})M_{\star}\|+\|E\|\lesssim c_{\delta}\kappa^{-2C_{\delta}/3}.

Thus all conclusion of Lemma
19
remains valid in the generalized setting.

Observation 2: In Phase II and III, the update equation has the same form as that in the noiseless setting.

Set

\Delta^{\prime}_{t}=\Delta_{t}-E,

then the update equation in the generalized setting can be expressed as

X_{t+1}=X_{t}-\eta(X_{t}X_{t}^{\top}-M_{\star})X_{t}(X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}+\eta\Delta^{\prime}_{t}X_{t}(X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1},

which has the same form with the noiseless update equation (
63
), if we replace
\Delta_{t}
there by
\Delta^{\prime}_{t}
. In the proof of Phase II, the only property of
\Delta_{t}
we used is (
50
), which still holds for
\Delta_{t}^{\prime}
since
\|E\|
is small. Thus the proof can be simply carried over to the generalized setting of Theorem
6
. Moreover, in the proof of Phase III, the only places that involve controlling
\Delta_{t}
in a different manner than (
50
) are (
121
) and (
139
). These equations require us to control
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}
for some unitarily invariant norm
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}
. If we replace
\Delta_{t}
by
\Delta^{\prime}_{t}
, we can bound in these equations that

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta^{\prime}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\leq{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}E|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}.

Since any unitarily invariant
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}
is bounded by the operator norm up to a multiplicative constant
5
5

5

In this paper,
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}
is always taken to be either the operator norm or the Frobenius norm, for which this assertion is elementarily obvious.
(depending on the rank of the matrix), we may control
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}E|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}
using the assumption (
142
).
Then we may combine (
121
) and (
139
) (assuming (
139
) also holds with the Frobenius norm replaced by
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}
) to obtain

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t+1}\widetilde{S}_{t+1}^{\top}-\Sigma_{\star}^{2})\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\|X_{\star}\|^{-1}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t+1}\widetilde{S}_{t+1}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left(1-\frac{\eta}{10}\right)\left({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma_{\star}^{2})\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|X_{\star}\|^{-1}\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\right)+\eta C\kappa^{4}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}E|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\eta\left(\frac{\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|}{\|X_{\star}\|}\right)^{1/2}.

(148)

The conclusion of the theorem would immediately follow from the above inequality combined with Lemma
29
and Lemma
27
, by taking
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}
to be the operator norm and the Frobenius norm.

Based on these observations, we formally state below the generalizations of key lemmas in the three phases required to prove Theorem
6
.
Most of them have identical proofs to their noiseless counterparts, and in such cases the proofs will be omitted. The few of them that require a slightly modified proof will be discussed in full detail.

G.1
Generalization of Phase I

Our goal is to prove Lemma
3
in the generalized setting.

Lemma 31
.

The conclusions of Lemma
3
, along with its corollaries (
3
) and (
24
), still hold in the setting of Theorem
6
.

As in the proof in the noiseless setting, this lemma is proved if we can prove the two parts of it respectively: the base case, where we show that there exists some
t_{1}\leq T_{\min}/16
such that (
21
) holds
and that (
22
) hold with
t=t_{1}
, and the induction step, where we show that (
22
) continues to hold for
t\in[t_{1},T_{\max}]
.

G.1.1
Establishing the base case

We first show that Lemma
19
still holds in the generalized setting.

Lemma 32
.

Under the same setting as Theorem
6
,
we have for some
t_{1}\leq T_{\min}/16
such that (
21
) holds
and that (
22
) hold with
t=t_{1}
.

We prove this result in a slightly more general setting. We consider a general symmetric matrix
\widehat{M}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
, and set

\widehat{X}_{t}=\left(I+\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\widehat{M}\right)^{t}X_{0},\quad t=0,1,2,\cdots

We also denote

\displaystyle s_{j}\coloneqq\sigma_{j}\Big(I+\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\widehat{M}\Big)=1+\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\sigma_{j}\big(\widehat{M}\big),\qquad j=1,2,\ldots,n

The treatment of the noiseless setting in Appendix
C
corresponds to the special case
\widehat{M}=\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})
. In the generalized setting, we choose
\widehat{M}=\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})+E
. The following two lemmas are generalized from the lemmas in Appendix
C
, but have verbatim proofs as those, which are therefore omitted.

Lemma 33

(Generalization of Lemma
20
)
.

Suppose that
\lambda\geq\frac{1}{100}c_{\lambda}\sigma_{\min}^{2}(X_{\star})
. For any
\theta\in(0,1)
, there exists a large enough constant
K=K(\theta,c_{\lambda},C_{G})>0
such that the following holds. As long as
\alpha
obeys

\displaystyle\log\frac{\|X_{\star}\|}{\alpha}\geq\frac{K}{\eta}\log(2\kappa n)\cdot\Big(1+\log\Big(1+\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\|\widehat{M}\|\Big)\Big),

(149)

one has for all
t\leq\frac{1}{\theta\eta}\log(\kappa n)
:

\displaystyle\big\lVert X_{t}-\widehat{X}_{t}\big\rVert

\displaystyle\leq t\Big(1+\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\lVert\widehat{M}\rVert\Big)^{t}\frac{\alpha^{2}}{\|X_{\star}\|}.

(150)

Moreover,
\lVert X_{t}\rVert\leq\lVert X_{\star}\rVert
for all such
t
.

Lemma 34

(Generalization of Lemma
21
)
.

There exists some small universal constant
c_{\ref{lem:noisy-Mahdi}}>0
such that the following hold.
Assume that for some
\gamma\leq c_{\ref{lem:noisy-Mahdi}}
,

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{M}-M_{\star}\rVert\leq\gamma\sigma_{\min}^{2}(X_{\star}),

(151)

and furthermore,

\displaystyle\phi\coloneqq\frac{\alpha\|G\|s_{r_{\star}+1}^{t}+\|X_{t}-\widehat{X}_{t}\|}{\alpha\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{U}^{\top}G)s_{r_{\star}}^{t}}\leq c_{\ref{lem:noisy-Mahdi}}\kappa^{-2}.

(152)

Then for some universal
C_{\ref{lem:noisy-Mahdi}}>0
the following hold:

\displaystyle\sigma_{\min}(\widetilde{S}_{t})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\alpha}{4}\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{U}^{\top}G)s_{r_{\star}}^{t},

(153a)

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\ref{lem:noisy-Mahdi}}\phi\alpha\sigma_{\min}(\widehat{U}^{\top}G)s_{r_{\star}}^{t},

(153b)

\displaystyle\|U_{{\star,\perp}}^{\top}U_{\widetilde{X}_{t}}\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\ref{lem:noisy-Mahdi}}(\gamma+\phi),

(153c)

where
\widetilde{X}_{t}\coloneqq X_{t}V_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times r_{\star}}
.

We are now ready to prove Lemma
32
.

Proof of Lemma
32
.

Recall that the generalized setting corresponds to
\widehat{M}=\mathcal{A^{*}A}(M_{\star})+E
.
The proof is mostly identical to the proof of Lemma
19
. Similar to that proof, we first need to verify the two assumptions in Lemma
34
. The rest of the proof goes exactly the same, thus is omitted here.

Verifying assumption (
151
).

By the RIP in (
9
), Lemma
8
, the condition of
\delta
in (
10
), and the assumption (
142
), we have

\displaystyle\|\widehat{M}-M_{\star}\|=\big\lVert(\id-\mathcal{A^{*}A})(M_{\star})+E\big\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{r_{\star}}\delta\|M_{\star}\|+c_{\sigma}\kappa^{-C_{\sigma}}\|M_{\star}\|

\displaystyle\leq c_{\delta}\kappa^{-(C_{\delta}-2)}\sigma_{\min}^{2}(X_{\star})+c_{\sigma}\kappa^{-(C_{\sigma}-2)}\sigma_{\min}^{2}(X_{\star})

\displaystyle\eqqcolon\gamma\sigma_{\min}^{2}(X_{\star}).

(154)

Here
\gamma=c_{\delta}\kappa^{-(C_{\delta}-2)}+c_{\sigma}\kappa^{-(C_{\sigma}-2)}\leq c_{\ref{lem:Mahdi}}
, as

c_{\delta}
and
c_{\sigma}
are assumed to be sufficiently small.

Verifying assumption (
152
).

By Weyl’s inequality and (
G.1.1
), we have

\displaystyle\Big|s_{j}-1-\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\sigma_{j}(M_{\star})\Big|\leq\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\big\lVert\widehat{M}-M_{\star}\big\rVert\leq\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\gamma\sigma_{\min}^{2}(X_{\star})\leq\frac{100(c_{\delta}+c_{\sigma})}{c_{\lambda}}\eta,

where the last inequality follows from the condition
\lambda\geq\frac{1}{100}c_{\lambda}\sigma_{\min}^{2}(X_{\star})
.
Furthermore, using the condition
\lambda\leq c_{\lambda}\sigma_{\min}^{2}(X_{\star})
assumed in (
12b
),
the above bound implies that, for some
C=C(c_{\lambda},c_{\sigma},c_{\delta})>0
,

\displaystyle s_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 1+\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\|M_{\star}\|+\frac{100(c_{\delta}+c_{\sigma})}{c_{\lambda}}\eta\leq 1+C\eta\kappa^{2},

(155a)

\displaystyle s_{r_{\star}}

\displaystyle\geq 1+\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\sigma_{\min}^{2}(X_{\star})-\frac{100(c_{\delta}+c_{\sigma})}{c_{\lambda}}\eta\geq 1+\frac{\eta}{2c_{\lambda}},

(155b)

\displaystyle s_{r_{\star}}

\displaystyle\leq 1+\frac{\eta}{\lambda}\sigma_{\min}^{2}(X_{\star})+\frac{100(c_{\delta}+c_{\sigma})}{c_{\lambda}}\eta\leq 1+\frac{2\eta}{c_{\lambda}},

(155c)

\displaystyle s_{r_{\star}+1}

\displaystyle\leq 1+\frac{100(c_{\delta}+c_{\sigma})}{c_{\lambda}}\eta\leq 1+\frac{\eta}{4c_{\lambda}},

(155d)

where we use the fact that
\sigma_{r_{\star}+1}(M_{\star})=0
, and
c_{\delta}+c_{\sigma}\leq 1/400
.
The rest of the verification is the same as the verification of (
80
) in the proof of Lemma
19
.
∎

G.1.2
Establishing the induction step

Following the proof of the noiseless setting, we would like to show that Lemmas
23
,
24
,
25
still hold in the generalized setting, which in turn relies entirely on Lemmas
13
,
14
,
15
. Since Lemma
14
and Lemma
15
are both corollaries of Lemma
13
, it suffices to prove the generalization of Lemma
13
in the generalized setting.

Lemma 35

(Generalization of Lemma
13
)
.

Assume the update equation of
X_{t}
has the following form (cf. (
63
)):

X_{t+1}=X_{t}-\eta(X_{t}X_{t}^{\top}-M_{\star})X_{t}(X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1}+\eta\Delta^{\prime}_{t}X_{t}(X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}+\lambda I)^{-1},

where
\Delta^{\prime}_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
is some symmetric matrix satisfying
\|\Delta^{\prime}_{t}\|\leq c_{\ref{lem:Delta-bound}}\kappa^{-2C_{\delta}/3}\|X_{\star}\|^{2}
.
For any
t
such that
\widetilde{S}_{t}
is invertible and (
22
) holds, the equations (
53a
) and (
53b
) hold, where error terms are bounded by (
54b
)–(
54d
) and the following modifications of (
54a
) and (
54e
):

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{E}^{a}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\leq 2c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}\kappa^{-4}\|X_{\star}\|\cdot{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+2{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta^{\prime}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|},

(156a)

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|{E}^{e}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\leq 2{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta^{\prime}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+c_{\ref{lem:Delta-bound}}\kappa^{-5}\|X_{\star}\|\cdot{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}.

(156b)

The proof is verbatim to Lemma
13
.
Note that the noiseless setting corresponds to the special case
\Delta^{\prime}_{t}=\Delta_{t}=(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{A^{*}A})(M_{\star})
, while the generalized setting corresponds to
\Delta^{\prime}_{t}=\Delta_{t}-E=(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{A^{*}A})(M_{\star})-E
. To show that Lemma
35
is applicable to the generalized setting we need to verify that this choice of
\Delta^{\prime}_{t}
guarantees the smallness of
\|\Delta^{\prime}_{t}\|
, which is proved in the following lemma.

Lemma 36

(Generalization of (
50
) in Lemma
12
)
.

Under the same setting as Theorem
6
, for any
t
such that (
22
) holds, we have

\|\Delta^{\prime}_{t}\|\leq c_{\ref{lem:Delta-bound}}\kappa^{-2C_{\delta}/3}\|X_{\star}\|^{2}.

Proof.

Combining (
52
) in the proof of Lemma
12
the assumption
\|E\|\leq c_{\sigma}\kappa^{-C_{\sigma}}\|M_{\star}\|
in (
142
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|\Delta^{\prime}_{t}\|

\displaystyle\leq 16\delta\sqrt{r_{\star}}\kappa^{2}(C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}^{2}+1)\|X_{\star}\|^{2}+c_{\sigma}\kappa^{-C_{\sigma}}\|X_{\star}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq(16c_{\delta}\kappa^{-C_{\delta}+2}(C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}^{2}+1)^{2}+c_{\sigma}\kappa^{-C_{\sigma}})\|X_{\star}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq c_{\ref{lem:Delta-bound}}\kappa^{-2C_{\delta}/3}\|X_{\star}\|^{2},

if we choose
C_{\sigma}\geq C_{\delta}
,
c_{\sigma}\leq c_{\delta}
, and note that
c_{\ref{lem:Delta-bound}}=32(C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}+1)^{2}c_{\delta}
as defined in Lemma
12
(please refer to the argument after (
52
) for details).
∎

With these fundamental results in hand we can follow the same arguments as in the noiseless case to prove the following generalization of the lemmas in Appendix
C.2
.

Lemma 37
.

The conclusions of Lemmas
23
,
25
still hold in the setting of Theorem
6
. Moreover, the following modification of Lemma
24
holds in the setting of Theorem
6
.
For any
t
such that (
22
) holds,
setting
Z_{t}=\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}+\lambda I)\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}
,
there exists some universal constant
C_{\ref{lem:misalign-update}}>0
such that

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t+1}\widetilde{S}_{t+1}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\leq\left(1-\frac{\eta}{3(\|Z_{t}\|+\eta)}\right){|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\eta\frac{C_{\ref{lem:misalign-update}}\kappa^{2}}{c_{\lambda}\|X_{\star}\|}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta^{\prime}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\eta\left(\frac{\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|}{\sigma_{\min}(\widetilde{S}_{t})}\right)^{1/2}\|X_{\star}\|.

(157)

In particular, if
c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}=100C_{\ref{lem:misalign-update}}(C_{\ref{lem:p1.5}.a}+1)^{4}c_{\delta}/c_{\lambda}
, then

\|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|\leq c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}\kappa^{-C_{\delta}/2}\|X_{\star}\|
implies

\|\widetilde{N}_{t+1}\widetilde{S}_{t+1}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|\leq c_{\ref{lem:p1.5}}\kappa^{-C_{\delta}/2}\|X_{\star}\|
.

By the arguments following Lemma
25
,
the above results are sufficient to prove the induction step, thereby completing the proof of Lemma
3
in the generalized setting.

G.2
Generalization of Phase II

We will prove Lemma
4
and Lemma
5
, the main results of Phase II, in the generalized setting.

Lemma 38
.

The conclusions of Lemma
4
and Lemma
5
, along with Corollary
1
and Corollary
2
, still hold under the generalized setting of Theorem
6
.

Tracking the proof of Phase II in Appendix
D
, one may verify that all proofs there hold verbatim in the generalized setting, with Lemma
35
in place of Lemma
13
(the proof also used Lemmas
14
,
15
, which are corollaries of Lemma
13
, hence hold in the generalized setting given Lemma
35
), except for Lemma
26
, which should be substituted by the following generalization:

Lemma 39
.

Under the same setting as Theorem
6
, for any
t:t_{2}\leq t\leq T_{\max}
, one has

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Gamma_{t+1}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\leq(1-\eta){|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Gamma_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\eta\frac{C_{\ref{lem:local-unify}}\kappa^{4}}{\|X_{\star}\|^{2}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}\Delta^{\prime}_{t}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\frac{1}{16}\eta\|X_{\star}\|^{-1}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\eta\left(\frac{\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|}{\|X_{\star}\|}\right)^{7/12},

(158)

where
C_{\ref{lem:local-unify}}\lesssim c_{\lambda}^{-1/2}
is some positive constant and
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}
can either be the Frobenius norm or the spectral norm.

The proof is identical to that of Lemma
26
, thus is omitted here. Following the proof in Appendix
D
, these generalized results are sufficient to prove Lemma
38
, thereby completing the proof of Phase II in the generalized setting.

G.3
Generalization of Phase III

Our goal is to prove the following modification of Lemma
6
in the generalized setting.

Lemma 40

(Generalization of Lemma
6
)
.

Under the same setting as Theorem
6
, there exists some universal constant
c_{\ref{lem:noisy-p3}}>0
such that
for any
t:t_{3}\leq t\leq T_{\max}
, with
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}
taken to be the operator norm
\|\cdot\|
or the Frobenius norm
\|\cdot\|_{\fro}
, we have

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|X_{t}X_{t}^{\top}-M_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\leq(1-c_{\ref{lem:noisy-p3}}\eta)^{t-t_{3}}r_{\star}\|M_{\star}\|+c_{\ref{lem:noisy-p3}}^{-1}\kappa^{4}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}E|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+8c_{\ref{lem:noisy-p3}}^{-1}\|M_{\star}\|\max_{t_{3}\leq\tau\leq t}\left(\frac{\|\widetilde{O}_{\tau}\|}{\|X_{\star}\|}\right)^{1/2}.

(159)

In particular, there exists an iteration number

t_{4}:t_{3}\leq t_{4}\leq t_{3}+T_{\min}/16
such that
for any
t\in[t_{4},T_{\max}]
, we have

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|X_{t}X_{t}^{\top}-M_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\leq\max(\alpha^{1/3}\|X_{\star}\|^{5/3},c_{\ref{lem:noisy-p3}}^{-1}\kappa^{4}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}E|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|})\leq\max(\varepsilon\|M_{\star}\|,c_{\ref{lem:noisy-p3}}^{-1}\kappa^{4}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}E|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}).

(160)

Here,
\varepsilon
and
\alpha
are as stated in Theorem
2
.

As in Appendix
E
, this will be accomplished by decomposing the error
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|X_{t}X_{t}^{\top}-M_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}
using Lemma
27
, and then control the components in the decomposition using Lemma
28
. It is easy to check that the proof of Lemma
27
applies without modification to the generalized setting, and in fact works with the Frobenius norm replaced by any unitarily invariant norm. This leads to the following generalization.

Lemma 41

(Generalization of Lemma
27
)
.

Under the same setting as Theorem
6
, for all
t\geq t_{3}
, as long as

\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma_{\star}^{2})\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\|\leq 1/10
,
one has

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|X_{t}X_{t}^{\top}-M_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\leq 4\|X_{\star}\|^{2}\left({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma_{\star}^{2})\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\|X_{\star}\|^{-1}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\right)+4\|X_{\star}\|\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|.

It remains to prove the generalization of Lemma
28
, stated below.

Lemma 42

(Generalization of Lemma
28
)
.

Under the same setting as Theorem
6
, there exists some universal constant
C_{\ref{lem:noisy-p3-aux}}>0
such that for any
t:t_{3}\leq t\leq T_{\max}
,
with
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}
taken to be the operator norm
\|\cdot\|
or the Frobenius norm
\|\cdot\|_{\fro}
,
one has

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t+1}\widetilde{S}_{t+1}^{\top}-\Sigma_{\star}^{2})\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\|X_{\star}\|^{-1}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t+1}\widetilde{S}_{t+1}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left(1-\frac{\eta}{10}\right)\left({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma_{\star}^{2})\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\|X_{\star}\|^{-1}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\right)+\eta\frac{C_{\ref{lem:noisy-p3-aux}}\kappa^{4}}{\|X_{\star}\|^{2}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}E|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\eta\left(\frac{\|\widetilde{O}_{t}\|}{\|X_{\star}\|}\right)^{1/2}.

(161)

In particular,
\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t+1}\widetilde{S}_{t+1}^{\top}-\Sigma_{\star}^{2})\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\|\leq 1/10

for all
t
such that
t_{3}\leq t\leq T_{\max}
.

We are prepared to formally prove Lemma
40
. Similar to the noiseless setting, we apply Lemma
42
repeatedly to obtain the following bound
for all
t\in[t_{3},T_{\max}]
:

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{\top}-\Sigma_{\star}^{2})\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\|X_{\star}\|^{-1}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t}\widetilde{S}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left(1-\frac{\eta}{10}\right)^{t-t_{3}}\left({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t_{3}}\widetilde{S}_{t_{3}}^{\top}-\Sigma_{\star}^{2})\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+\|X_{\star}\|^{-1}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{N}_{t_{3}}\widetilde{S}_{t_{3}}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\phantom{\leq}+\frac{10C_{\ref{lem:noisy-p3-aux}}\kappa^{4}}{\|X_{\star}\|^{2}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|U_{\star}^{\top}E|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}+10\max_{t_{3}\leq\tau\leq t}\left(\frac{\|\widetilde{O}_{\tau}\|}{\|X_{\star}\|}\right)^{1/2},

(162)

which motivates us to control the error at time
t_{3}
. With the same arguments as in the noiseless setting (cf. Equation (
136
) in Appendix
E
), we obtain

\|\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}(\widetilde{S}_{t_{3}}\widetilde{S}_{t_{3}}^{\top}-\Sigma_{\star}^{2})\Sigma_{\star}^{-1}\|_{\fro}+\|X_{\star}\|^{-1}\|\widetilde{N}_{t_{3}}\widetilde{S}_{t_{3}}^{-1}\Sigma_{\star}\|_{\fro}\leq\frac{\sqrt{r_{\star}}}{5}.

Since the operator norm of a matrix is always less than or equal to the Frobenius norm of it, the above inequality also holds if the Frobenius norm is replaced by the operator norm. Recalling that in this lemma,
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}
is taken to be either the operator norm or the Frobenius norm, we have shown
